\emph{\textbf{Definition 4.11.1 (Structural Grain).}}

For container C, define its structural grain as a trailing,
coupling-weighted average of the unit vectors already associated with
its own past strikes and couplings:

\textbf{g}\_C(t) = normalize{[} ∫₀ᵗ K(t,s) · Σ\_k
Λ\_kC(s)·\textbf{û}\_kC(s) ds {]},

using §6.7's existing memory kernel K(t,s) and §8.8a's existing per-edge
unit vectors û\_kj, aggregated over C's own coupling history rather than
over a single simultaneous multi-attacker event as Drive\_B does. Where
a container has no coupling history (the trailing integral is zero),
g\_C(t) is undefined and the directional modifier below defaults to
neutral (χ\_dir = 1): a container without an established grain has no
basis for anything to be measured as orthogonal or aligned against, and
is treated as direction-agnostic until it accumulates one.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 4.11.2 (Directional Force Modifier).}}

For a Drive\_B(j,t) event (§8.8a) with net push direction
\textbf{û}\_net(j,t) = {[}Σ\_k Λ\_kj·\textbf{û}\_kj{]} / ‖Σ\_k
Λ\_kj·\textbf{û}\_kj‖ --- the normalized direction already implicit in
Drive\_B's own magnitude formula --- with the convention that where Σ\_k
Λ\_kj·\textbf{û}\_kj = 0 (simultaneous forces exactly cancel, leaving no
net directional push), \textbf{û}\_net(j,t) is undefined and χ\_dir(j,t)
defaults to 1, the same neutral convention already adopted for
\textbf{g}\_C(t)'s own zero-history case in Definition 4.11.1: no net
push means nothing for a grain to be orthogonal or aligned against, and
Drive\_B(j,t)'s own magnitude in this case is separately already zero,
so the event contributes no tipping momentum regardless. Otherwise,
define the alignment between the incoming push and the container's own
grain,

align(j,t) = \textbar g\_j(t) · û\_net(j,t)\textbar{} ∈ {[}0,1{]} ---
the absolute value is load-bearing, not decorative: the raw dot product
of two unit vectors ranges over {[}−1,1{]}, and without it, a push
exactly anti-parallel to the grain (align = −1) would compute as
maximally misaligned (χ\_dir = 1+2κ\_dir) rather than fully aligned,
contradicting the very next sentence's stated convention that either
direction along the grain counts as aligned.

and a directional force modifier,

χ\_dir(j,t) = 1 + κ\_dir·(1 − align(j,t)), κ\_dir ≥ 0 fitted.

χ\_dir(j,t) → 1 as the incoming push aligns with the container's
established grain (in either direction along it, since alignment is
measured by absolute value); χ\_dir(j,t) → 1 + κ\_dir as the push
approaches orthogonality to that grain, regardless of the push's own
absolute direction. This is the formal content of the pancaking claim:
what matters is the angle between shock and grain, not which way either
one points in absolute terms.

\hypertarget{why-the-cheap-path-and-the-cost-of-the-full-one}{%
\subsection{4.12 Why the Cheap Path, and the Cost of the Full
One}\label{why-the-cheap-path-and-the-cost-of-the-full-one}}

\textbf{Why this construction was affordable where a full directional
geometry was not.} §4.11's grain-and-modifier construction was cheap for
a specific, structural reason, not merely because less effort was spent
on it: a natural source of directional data already existed in the
document for an unrelated purpose --- Drive\_B's û\_kj unit vectors,
used in §8.8a to combine multiple simultaneous attackers into a single
push magnitude. Building g\_C(t) and χ\_dir(t) required no new geometric
primitive, only aggregating data already being collected. Just as
importantly, the application was scoped to exactly two formulas,
M\_tip(B1) and M\_tip(B2) (§10.5b.7) --- nothing about V(t), W(t), Axiom
C3 or C4, the entropy-regime classification, or any divergence formula
elsewhere in this document was touched. Setting κ\_dir = 0 recovers the
original undirected mathematics exactly, which is what makes this an
addition rather than a revision: everything built before this addendum
continues to hold unchanged.

\textbf{What a full directional geometry would actually require.} A
reader considering whether to build the fuller version --- separate
metrics for verticality and horizontality, or any construction giving
V(t)/W(t) themselves directional structure rather than modulating one
downstream momentum calculation --- should expect the following, not a
contained addition:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Axioms C3 and C4} (§10.5b.0) are stated in scalar terms ---
  verticality crossing a threshold, horizontality approaching its
  maximum as Π\_f → 1. Both would need restatement for a directional
  V(t), and any downstream result derived from their scalar form
  (Definition 10.5b.0.6's Ultimate Convergence, Definition 10.5b.0.8's
  sustained-state tipping-immunity check, stated as the scalar equality
  V(t) = h\_min) would need re-derivation, not just re-notation.
\item
  \textbf{The entropy-regime classification itself} (§10.5b.1) runs on
  scalar variance, σ\_o(t) and σ\_p(t). A directional account would need
  to specify what a ``direction'' of variance means for a probability
  distribution over Ω, which is not a small extension --- it is a new
  question the current framework's outcome-space ontology does not
  currently ask.
\item
  \textbf{Every scalar divergence comparison downstream} --- F\_boom and
  F\_demand (§10.5b.16.1, §10.5b.18.8) both compare scalar D\_op or
  D\_om values directly against thresholds. Since D\_ij is
  Jensen--Shannon divergence between distributions with no vector
  structure (§6.3), giving these comparisons directional meaning would
  mean redefining what divergence itself carries throughout Part 6, not
  adding a modifier term to Part 10 formulas that already consume a
  scalar D\_ij as given.
\item
  \textbf{Ultimately, Part 2's primitive objects.} Any of the above
  pursued fully eventually reaches back to how a container's basic
  informational state is represented at all, the deepest layer this
  document has --- the same layer-risk reasoning §4.11 raised before the
  cheap alternative was found, restated here because the fuller
  construction, if built, would not be exempt from it.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{When it would actually be worth doing.} Three concrete
conditions, not a general sense that more precision is always better:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Real data shows the directional effect matters somewhere §4.11's cheap
  version does not currently reach --- Flip Condition A, pre-flip
  disintegration (§10.5b.9d, which deliberately does not consume χ\_dir,
  since overload is a volume claim rather than a directional one), or
  the forcing terms F\_boom and F\_demand.
\item
  V(t) or W(t) themselves need to carry structure beyond what modulating
  a single downstream momentum calculation can express --- for instance,
  a claim that a container's \emph{degree} of rigidity, not merely its
  tipping-momentum accumulation rate, depends on shock direction.
\item
  Cross-container grain comparison becomes theoretically necessary ---
  comparing two containers' structural grains against each other, rather
  than one container's incoming shock against its own grain, which is
  all §4.11 currently supports.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{A related question resolved separately, RICD 15.8.} The
entropy-regime classification's own scalar variance, flagged just above
as one of the things a full directional geometry would need to address,
produced a real, observed failure in the higher-ed tracker (a resource
windfall misclassified as instability). That case is resolved in
Definition 10.5b.1a without requiring the fuller construction warned
against in this section: rather than giving P\_o/P\_p's own
divergence-based variance an intrinsic direction, the fix sources
direction externally, from d\_A(t)'s already-signed rate of change. The
deeper question raised in this section --- what a directional account of
variance over a probability distribution would even mean --- remains
exactly as open as stated above; it was not answered, only avoided for
this specific case by finding it was not the question that needed
answering.

None of these currently hold. This document already has one documented
instance of a structurally similar fuller move --- an earlier 90°
inversion operator for tipping outcomes --- being attempted and
abandoned as redundant with machinery that already determined the same
result without it (§10.5b.9's mass-weighted resolution). That precedent
does not guarantee a full directional geometry would meet the same fate,
but it is a real prior data point against assuming the investment would
obviously pay for itself, and is offered here as caution rather than
discouragement.

\hypertarget{part-5.-recursive-containers}{%
\section{Part 5. Recursive
Containers}\label{part-5.-recursive-containers}}

Adaptive systems exist within nested organizational structures. Cells
compose tissues, tissues compose organs, organs compose organisms,
organisms compose populations, and populations compose ecosystems.
Similarly, software modules compose applications, applications compose
distributed services, and services compose technological
infrastructures.

Recursive Information--Container Dynamics treats this nested
organization as a mathematical property rather than a modeling
convenience. Every container may simultaneously function as an
autonomous adaptive system, a component of a larger adaptive system, and
an environment for lower-level adaptive systems. The same mathematical
rules apply at every level of recursion.

\hypertarget{recursive-levels}{%
\subsection{5.1 Recursive Levels}\label{recursive-levels}}

Let C\^{}(k) denote the collection of containers at recursion level k.
Examples: neurons → neural assemblies → cortical regions → whole brain;
or programmers → software teams → engineering divisions → corporation.
No level possesses privileged mathematical status.

\hypertarget{aggregation-operator}{%
\subsection{5.2 Aggregation Operator}\label{aggregation-operator}}

Higher-order containers emerge through aggregation.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 5.1.}}

\emph{A}\_k : C\^{}(k) → C\^{}(k+1).

For \{C₁, \ldots, Cₙ\}, C* = A\_k(C₁, \ldots, Cₙ).

C* is not equal to the sum of the C\_i. Aggregation is not simple
addition; the resulting container possesses new organizational
structure.

\emph{\textbf{Aggregation-weight functional.}} The aggregation weight
w\_i is not raw R\_i itself: define w\_i = W\_A(R\_i, \ldots), an
aggregation-weight functional. Raw resources are not assumed
commensurable across children; the default instantiation of W\_A is
therefore based on each child's own §5.6-normalized capacity, N\_\{R,i\}
(fraction of that child's historical or designed maximum currently
available), rather than raw R\_i. Where children's resources are not
naturally comparable even after this normalization, a domain adapter may
supply its own weighting in place of the default.

The recursive aggregation \emph{architecture} --- that a parent's
properties are computed by weighting and combining children's, per
Definition 5.2 --- is universal across domains; the specific weighting
\emph{metric} instantiating W\_A is domain-specific and
adapter-supplied. These are separate claims: this document's
universality claim is about the architecture, not about any one domain's
choice of W\_A.

\textbf{Emergence Principle.}

Aggregation produces a higher-order state representation: the
aggregation operator generates a parent-level state that is not the sum
of its children's states, and is not itself reducible to any individual
component. Emergent higher-order \emph{variables} in the stronger sense
--- institutional culture, organizational policy, software architecture,
ecological niche, corporate governance, social norms --- arise when the
higher-order dynamics acting on that representation contain degrees of
organization not reducible to any individual child's own state
representation. These quantities belong to C\^{}(k+1), not to any
individual C\^{}(k); §5.2's default aggregation (below) establishes the
former claim mathematically, while the latter additionally requires the
network coupling dynamics of Part 8 (see the discussion following the
default instantiation).

\textbf{Computational instantiation of the aggregation operator A.}

Child containers generally do not share a state space:

Ω₁ ≠ Ω₂ ≠ \ldots{} in general.

For example, sibling containers C\_a, C\_b, and C\_c may operate with
distinct, incommensurable localized states. Consequently, the
aggregation operator A cannot be defined as a direct pooling of the
local probability distributions P\_i. Instead, the aggregation operator
is instantiated in two steps:

\textbf{1. Commensuration.} Each child container supplies a summary map

\emph{σ}\_i : \emph{𝒫}(Ω\_i) → Δ\^{}m

that projects its local outcome distribution onto a fixed, shared
m-category severity scale common to the entire recursive system, such as
a normalized baseline-deviation scale. Although the map σ\_i is
specified by the domain adapter for each individual container, its
codomain Δ\^{}m --- the m-category probability simplex --- must remain
identical for every child of a given parent.

\textbf{2. Resource-weighted pooling.} Once the local distributions are
commensurated and made comparable, the parent state is defined as a
weighted mixture over the shared simplex, using the aggregation-weight
functional w\_i already specified above rather than raw resource
fractions directly:

P* = Σᵢ wᵢ σᵢ(Pᵢ), wᵢ = W\_A(Rᵢ, \ldots) ≥ 0, normalized so Σᵢ wᵢ = 1
--- both conditions required jointly for P* to land in the shared
simplex Δ\^{}m rather than merely satisfying the summation constraint
alone.

Where every child has zero weight, Σᵢ W\_A(Rᵢ, \ldots) = 0 (for
instance, every child at zero capacity), the normalization is undefined
and P* is not formed: the parent has no capacity-bearing child to
aggregate, and is ineligible for weighted aggregation until some child
carries positive weight, unless the adapter declares equal weights for
that case.

Where children's resources are genuinely commensurable, W\_A defaults to
R\_i's §5.6-normalized form (N\_\{R,i\}), per the aggregation-weight
functional above, rather than raw, unnormalized R\_i.

This default instantiation of A constitutes a genuine aggregation rather
than a simple sum of the component distributions, establishing the
Emergence Principle's first claim, under a stated non-degeneracy
condition: P* is a new higher-order state representation, not identical
to any single component, whenever no positively weighted component's
projected distribution lies in the convex hull of the others'. Formally,
if P* = σ\_k(P\_k) for some k with w\_k \textless{} 1, then σ\_k(P\_k) =
Σ\_\{i≠k\} (w\_i / (1 − w\_k)) σ\_i(P\_i), a convex combination of the
other components; so where the projected distributions with w\_i
\textgreater{} 0 are affinely independent, P* ≠ σ\_k(P\_k) for every k.
In the degenerate case --- every positively weighted component carrying
the same projected distribution Q --- P* = Q exactly, and no new
representation arises. It does not, by itself, establish the second,
stronger claim --- that genuinely novel emergent variables (culture,
policy, architecture) arise from the aggregation alone. A convex mixture
of child distributions establishes only that the aggregate
representation is a distinct object from its parts; it does not
mathematically generate those higher-order variables. Fully emergent
structure, such as correlations induced by cross-container coupling,
additionally requires the network dynamics of Part 8 to feed into P*.

This default can be overridden by a domain-specific A\_k where
warranted, but P* must remain a genuine probability distribution over
some stated Ω* for every subsequent RICD construction---D\_ij, I, S(t),
etc.---to apply to C* unchanged.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 5.2a (Inheritance of Dynamical Form Under
Recursive Composition).}} Let a parent C* = A(C\_1, \ldots, C\_n) be
formed by the aggregation operator above, P* = Σ\_i w\_i·σ\_i(P\_i),
with children whose informational dynamics share the form of Part 6,
dP\_i/dt = F(P\_i; θ\_i) + Σ\_j Λ\_ij·G(P\_i, P\_j). Assume (H1)
commensuration carries the shared form into the severity simplex: there
is a field F* on Δ\^{}m with Jσ\_i·F(P\_i; θ\_i) = F*(σ\_i(P\_i); θ\_i),
and likewise G* for the coupling term, where Jσ\_i is the Jacobian of
child i's commensuration map; and (H2) F* and G* are twice continuously
differentiable in state and parameters, with second derivatives bounded
in norm by a constant K\_2 on a convex neighborhood of (P*, θ̄)
containing every (σ\_i(P\_i), θ\_i). Write ε = max\_i ‖σ\_i(P\_i) − P*‖
for the dispersion of the commensurated children about the parent state
and δ = max\_i ‖θ\_i − θ̄‖ for their parameter dispersion about the
weight-averaged θ̄ = Σ\_i w\_i·θ\_i. Then

dP*/dt = F*(P*; θ̄) + Λ̄·G*(P*, P*) + E(t) + O(ε² + δ²), with the
remainder bounded by c·K\_2·(1 + Λ̄)·(ε² + δ²) for a constant c depending
only on the norms used,

where Λ̄ = Σ\_\{i,j\} w\_i·Λ\_ij is the weight-averaged within-parent
coupling and E(t) = Σ\_i (dw\_i/dt)·σ\_i(P\_i) + Σ\_\{i,j\}
w\_i·Λ\_ij·{[}G*(σ\_i(P\_i), σ\_j(P\_j)) − G*(P*, P*){]} collects weight
drift and the part of internal coupling that does not reduce to a
function of P*. Proof sketch: differentiate P* = Σ\_i w\_i·σ\_i(P\_i),
apply (H1) to each child, and expand F*(σ\_i(P\_i); θ\_i) about (P*, θ̄);
the first-order terms vanish because Σ\_i w\_i·(σ\_i(P\_i) − P*) = 0 and
Σ\_i w\_i·(θ\_i − θ̄) = 0. The parent therefore inherits its children's
dynamical form, open or closed, with aggregated coefficients, whenever
dispersion is small and E(t) is small relative to F*. Where internal
coupling among children is strong, E(t) is not small, and the parent
acquires dynamics of its own, which this document models explicitly at
the parent level as grid dynamics (Part 8) rather than assuming away.
This is why open and closed dynamics carry from people up to the
institutions they compose, and why outcomes such as the tragedy of the
commons appear as grid-level collapse dynamics of the parent container
rather than as exceptions to the framework. The claim of
domain-generality rests on the recursion, which applies equally to cells
within tissues and populations within ecosystems, and not on the human
composition of any particular container.

\emph{Domain instances.} Firms whose staff operate in open, reciprocal
exchange tend to exhibit open institutional dynamics, and institutions
composed of closed, extractive units tend to exhibit closed ones; tissue
health reflects the aggregate state of its cells, and a tumor is the
case in which one subpopulation's dynamics dominate the residual;
Schelling's segregation model and the tragedy of the commons are cases
in which E(t) is large and grid-level dynamics must be modeled
explicitly.

\hypertarget{projection-operator}{%
\subsection{5.3 Projection Operator}\label{projection-operator}}

Higher-level organization constrains lower-level behavior: Π\_k :
C\^{}(k+1) → C\^{}(k). Examples include corporate policy affecting
employees, immune regulation affecting cells, operating systems
constraining software processes, and constitutions constraining
governmental agencies. Aggregation and projection form complementary
recursive operators.

\hypertarget{recursive-consistency}{%
\subsection{5.4 Recursive Consistency}\label{recursive-consistency}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 5.2.}}

A recursive hierarchy satisfies consistency when O(A(C\_i)) ≈
Γ(O(C\_i)), where Γ is an admissible aggregation rule defined on
observations. Perfect equality is neither expected nor desirable;
emergent systems necessarily generate new organizational information.

\hypertarget{boundary-nesting}{%
\subsection{5.5 Boundary Nesting}\label{boundary-nesting}}

\textbf{Correction.}

Sibling containers C\_i are not subsets of the parent aggregate C*.
Set-containment nesting applies only to strict-subset recursion (e.g.,
localized sub-compartments nested inside a larger container that is
itself one of the aggregated children). For aggregation-type recursion,
the correct relation is compatibility, not containment: define B\_i ⊑ B*
(``B\_i is boundary-compatible with the parent'') to mean every
interaction admitted by B\_i is also admissible under B*'s exchange
rules with C\_i's environment --- i.e., the parent's boundary operator
does not forbid anything the child's boundary operator permits toward
the parent. This does not imply identical permeability in either
recursion type --- a department may possess more permeable informational
boundaries than the corporation that contains it, and a sibling
container may possess more permeable boundaries than the aggregate.

\hypertarget{resource-flow-across-levels}{%
\subsection{5.6 Resource Flow Across
Levels}\label{resource-flow-across-levels}}

Let R\^{}(k) denote resource allocation at level k, and T\_R the
recursive transfer operator: R\^{}(k+1) = T\_R(R\^{}(k)). Higher-order
systems redistribute resources downward through T\_R⁻¹. Examples include
budgets, nutrients, computational allocation, attention, labor, and
bandwidth.

\textbf{Normalization requirement.}

Σ\_i R\_i\^{}(k) in the capacity accounting below presupposes R\_i is
expressed in a common unit across all children --- false in general for
aggregation-type recursion, since individual containers may measure
resources in entirely different physical or abstract units
(throughput-hours, capacity-years, budget-credits). Before summing, each
child's resource measure must pass through a normalization operator
N\_\{R,i\} : ℝ≥0 → {[}0,1{]}, typically utilization-based ---

\emph{N}\_R,i(R\_i) = R\_i / R\_i\^{}max,

i.e., fraction of that container's own maximum historical or designed
capacity currently available --- so that the capacity accounting below
is applied to normalized capacity shares, not raw incommensurable
quantities. R\_i\^{}max \textgreater{} 0 is an admissibility condition
for this normalization, not something guaranteed by the framework
itself: an adapter supplying R\_i\^{}max = 0 for a genuinely empty or
degenerate container leaves N\_R,i undefined by division, and such a
container is not eligible for normalized recursive aggregation under
this default until the adapter either supplies a positive capacity scale
or an alternative, explicitly stated normalization for that case.

\textbf{Normalized Capacity Accounting.}

\emph{Σᵢ N\_R,i(R\_i\^{}(k)) − L\^{}(k) = R*\^{}(k+1), L\^{}(k) ≥ 0 (in
normalized capacity units),}

This is an accounting identity in normalized capacity units, child-level
full-capacity equivalents, not conservation of any underlying physical
or economic quantity. Where a conserved aggregate is intended, the
adapter normalizes children against the parent's own resource scale and
states how the child scales map into it.

where L\^{}(k) ≥ 0 represents transfer losses, including entropy,
inefficiency, communication overhead, corruption, and leakage. Losses
are subtracted because capacity lost in transfer does not reach the
parent, so larger losses give a smaller parent. R*\^{}(k+1), Σᵢ N\_R,i
and L\^{}(k) are all in normalized capacity units, child-level
full-capacity equivalents, and the identity is dimensionally consistent
only in those units. Recursion naturally introduces dissipative effects.
If a genuinely common unit exists for a given application (e.g., a
single agency's sub-budgets, all in dollars), N\_\{R,i\} may be taken as
the identity and the original unnormalized form is recovered as a
special case.

\hypertarget{recursive-information-fields}{%
\subsection{5.7 Recursive Information
Fields}\label{recursive-information-fields}}

Each recursion level supports its own information field P\^{}(k).
Together these form \{P\^{}(0), P\^{}(1), \ldots\}. An employee, a
department, and an entire corporation all possess legitimate information
states; none is reducible to another.

\hypertarget{cross-scale-coupling}{%
\subsection{5.8 Cross-Scale Coupling}\label{cross-scale-coupling}}

Λ\_kl : C\^{}(k) → C\^{}(l) describes how disturbances propagate between
scales. Examples: a local software bug becoming a system outage,
employee burnout becoming organizational decline, species extinction
altering ecosystems. Λ\_kl ≠ Λ\_lk in general --- upward and downward
influence generally differ, so recursive dynamics are asymmetric.

\hypertarget{fixed-points-of-recursion}{%
\subsection{5.9 Fixed Points of
Recursion}\label{fixed-points-of-recursion}}

A recursive fixed point satisfies A ∘ Π(C) = C. Such systems reproduce
their own organizational structure across recursive scales --- stable
institutions, mature ecosystems, robust software architectures, healthy
biological development. Collapse corresponds not merely to informational
divergence, but to the destruction of recursive fixed-point structure.

\hypertarget{the-recursive-principle}{%
\subsection{5.10 The Recursive
Principle}\label{the-recursive-principle}}

Adaptive complexity arises not from the existence of many interacting
components, but from the continual bidirectional coupling between
organizational levels through recursive aggregation, projection, and
resource exchange. Information moves upward. Constraints move downward.
Resources move in both directions. Memory propagates across levels.

\hypertarget{part-6.-feedback-divergence-field-mechanics-fdfm}{%
\section{Part 6. Feedback Divergence Field Mechanics
(FDFM)}\label{part-6.-feedback-divergence-field-mechanics-fdfm}}

FDFM concerns only the evolution of informational representations within
a single bounded container. It describes how an adaptive system
constructs internal representations, compares those representations with
observed reality, updates them through feedback, and accumulates
informational stress when those updates become progressively impaired.

\hypertarget{internal-information-state}{%
\subsection{6.1 Internal Information
State}\label{internal-information-state}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 6.1 (Representation Operators).}}

Define two projection operators O\_o, O\_p, producing P\_o = O\_o(P),
P\_p = O\_p(P) --- official representation and operational
representation, respectively, both deterministic projections of the same
underlying informational state P.

\emph{Regularity.} The gradient flow of §6.4 differentiates Φ\_t through
O\_o and O\_p, so the default formulation requires both operators to be
continuously differentiable on the interior of 𝒫(Ω), with locally
Lipschitz Jacobians so that the flow is well-posed. Where an adapter's
operators are only Lipschitz or piecewise defined, the gradient is
replaced by an element of the Clarke subdifferential of Φ\^{}G\_t, and
the discrete update of §6.4 becomes a projected subgradient step with
the same projection. Where they are discontinuous, the adapter declares
a derivative-free update, such as descent on a smoothed surrogate of
Φ\^{}G\_t, and the outputs carry that declaration (§15.2).

Several claims. Where an adapter reads the official and operational
layers along several claim dimensions, the operators are applied one
dimension at a time, each landing in that dimension's own outcome space
(finite by default; an adapter using an infinite space states the
topology and regularity conditions Definition 6.3.4 requires), with the
regularity stated above for point claims (Definition 6.3.4); the
regularity a set claim needs for the gradient flow is not supplied by
the continuity of the set and is stated separately (Proposition 6.3.4e).
On a dimension where the official claim is stated as a range, the
official projection is set-valued, its value a non-empty, closed, convex
set of distributions, and divergences from it are distances to that set
(Definition 6.3.4a). With one dimension and a point claim this is the
definition above.

P\_m, the meta-representation, is not a third projection of P. It is an
emergent aggregate over the meta-representation layer's own population
of sub-processes, each holding its own local view informed by partial
exposure to P\_o and P\_p rather than direct access to P itself:

P\_m(t) = Σ\_k φ\_k(t) · P\_m\^{}(k)(t),

with φ\_k(t) ≥ 0 and Σ\_k φ\_k(t) = 1, which is what keeps P\_m(t) a
probability distribution.

where P\_m\^{}(k)(t) is the k-th sub-process's own locally-held
representation, and φ\_k(t) is that sub-process's share of the
population's aggregate weight at time t. The population-level
composition variables defined in §10.5b.18 --- ω\_meta\^{}up(t)
(Definition 10.5b.18.2) and the catalog's ω\_meta\^{}down(t) (Definition
10.5b.18.5), and Ψ\_loyal(t) (Definition 10.5b.18.12) --- are summary
statistics of exactly this φ\_k(t) weighting, restated at the level of
behavioral category (transmitting or not, conditional or unconditional
closure) rather than at the level of individual sub-processes.

The meta-representation channel must be sourced independently at the
level of data collection: as an emergent aggregate over its own
population, gathered through its own dedicated observation process,
rather than algebraically derived from the gap between P\_o and P\_p ---
so that D\_om and D\_pm can carry distinct diagnostic meaning in §6.3
and §10.5b, rather than being redundant with D\_op by construction.
Constructing P\_m this way, as a population aggregate rather than a
projection operator, gives this independence a form no projection
operator could deliver on its own: an emergent aggregate is
definitionally not an algebraic combination of the other two layers'
outputs. P\_m(t), so constructed, remains a well-formed distribution
over Ω, and D\_om, D\_pm remain ordinary applications of §6.3's
divergence to P\_m against P\_o and P\_p respectively.

\emph{What independence is required.} Three senses of independence
should not be conflated: independent data collection, independent
mathematical construction, and statistical independence. The
three-channel diagnosis requires the first two and not the third. P\_m
is informed by partial exposure to P\_o and P\_p, so its errors may be
correlated with theirs, and correlation is acceptable. What the
diagnosis requires is a non-degenerate error structure: writing the
measurement errors of the three channels as ε\_o, ε\_p and ε\_m, ε\_m
must not lie in the span of ε\_o and ε\_p, equivalently the joint error
covariance Σ\_ε of (ε\_o, ε\_p, ε\_m) must be nonsingular. Where Σ\_ε is
singular, D\_om and D\_pm are determined by D\_op and carry no
additional information; where it is nonsingular, the three divergences
are three distinct sources of evidence even when they are correlated. An
adapter supplying P\_m states which of the routes of §6.4 it uses and,
where it can, an estimate of Σ\_ε or of the rank of its sample
counterpart.

The meta-representation layer is better understood as a barometer of
systemic self-deception than as any kind of auditor: it is internal to
the container by definition --- what the container's own information
state does when official and operational reality are compared to each
other --- and its reading is accurate by degree, not by kind. A low
reading (§10.5b.18.2's low-entropy baseline, ω\_meta\^{}up(t) ≈ 1, the
layer functioning not as a firewall but as a permeable membrane ---
still present, still maintaining the separation between official and
operational that makes their comparison possible at all, but passing
information freely in both directions rather than obstructing it;
Proposition 10.5b.1.6) and a high reading (either non-baseline regime, a
meta layer typically split about evenly, with a substantial comfort- or
demand-aligned part) are the same instrument reporting different
underlying states, not a functioning instrument in one case and a broken
one in the other. A closed channel should not be assumed corrupt by
default: §10.5b.18's fuller account treats closure in both non-baseline
regimes as more often a protective response --- a population, entropic
or negentropic in composition, shielding the container from a shock it
is not yet positioned to absorb --- than evidence of a captured or
malfunctioning instrument, though a genuinely entropic-aligned
population, whose closure is unconditional, remains possible and is not
excluded by this reading. The full account of what determines a given
channel's reading is regime-dependent and given in §10.5b.18 and its
extension (§10.5b.18.5--18.31).

\textbf{Belief, in structural terms.} Throughout this document, a layer
is said to believe a proposition when the proposition is part of the
content its representation carries and its updates act on it; a
container believes what the layer holding its working account of itself
carries. A representation is sincere when it is produced by the layer's
own local processing rather than supplied from outside it (§10.5b.18.1).
Neither term implies awareness, intent or experience, and both apply to
containers with no self-model. A transformed cell whose surface
signaling continues to present normal self while its metabolism is
turned to its own replication believes its official story in exactly
this sense, as does a service node whose health check keeps reporting
success while its internal processes deadlock. Where a passage needs the
experiential sense, it says so and refers to §10.5b.19.10.

Definition 6.1.1 (External Governance Attestation). P\_m is internal by
construction --- an aggregate over the container's own population, per
the definition above --- and everything else in this document that bears
on it, whether fitted continuous features or the divergence measures
built from P\_m directly, is calibrated statistically, requiring enough
real positive examples to earn a weight. A genuinely different class of
evidence exists alongside these: a real, independent, external body's
own confirmed finding that a container's governance or self-monitoring
function has failed by its own established standards --- academic
freedom or shared-governance censure by a recognized professional body,
for a concrete instance already in use in this document's own adapter
work, but structurally general to any domain with an analogous
independent standards body. Where an adapter supplies such an
attestation, α\_gov(t) ∈ \{0,1\}, it should not be treated as one
continuous input among several requiring enough positive cases to be
statistically validated the way δR(t) or a domain-specific staffing
ratio would be --- the validation has already been performed, by the
external body itself, applying its own established, independent
standards, prior to and separately from anything this document's own
machinery computes. This is analogous to G(t)'s reliance on an external,
uncaptured party (§6.3.2) rather than internal agreement, but sharper:
G(t) requires an ongoing accuracy comparison this document must itself
construct and interpret; α\_gov(t) = 1 is a completed, external,
independent verdict, requiring no further statistical interpretation to
be treated as strong evidence of genuine governance dysfunction. Its
rarity in a given adapter's data (a low base rate is expected and
appropriate, not a data quality problem) is precisely why it cannot and
should not be fit as a weighted continuous feature; it should instead be
reported directly, as confirmed evidence, wherever an adapter can supply
it, regardless of how few or many other instances exist in the same
dataset to compare it against. A further, real requirement applies
specifically in domains where deliberate deception is structurally
possible, distinct from the higher-education case where the attesting
body's own genuine independence can ordinarily be taken as given:
α\_gov(t) = 1's evidentiary force depends entirely on the attesting
party's actual independence, and in an adversarial domain that
independence cannot itself be an assumed input --- it must be a
separately verified observable before the attestation is treated as
external evidence at all. A nominal oversight structure can itself be
fabricated or captured as part of the same deception the attestation is
meant to catch --- purported board members who do not exist, or who have
never heard of the organization they supposedly govern, are a real,
documented instance of exactly this failure mode. An adapter operating
in a domain where this is possible must verify the attesting body's own
genuine existence and independence as a precondition for supplying
α\_gov(t) = 1 at all, rather than treating the attestation's mere
existence in available records as sufficient; where that verification
cannot be performed, the correct value is α\_gov(t) = unmeasured
(§15.2's confidence-typing convention), not a default of 0 or 1.
Computational order of operations, stated explicitly rather than left to
be inferred from priority alone: (1) the adapter produces its comparable
continuous quantities; (2) the engine computes the continuous assessment
from those quantities exactly as it would with no attestation available;
(3) α\_gov(t) is checked separately, as its own distinct step, not
folded into (2)'s computation as an additional input feature; (4) where
α\_gov(t) = 1, that verdict supersedes step (2)'s output entirely for
the final label, rather than being blended, weighted, or averaged with
it; where α\_gov(t) = 0 or unmeasured, step (2)'s continuous assessment
stands as the final result unmodified. This sequence, not merely the
priority relationship already established above, is what an
implementation must actually follow.

\emph{\textbf{Confirmed directly against real data: fitting versus
overriding, tested both ways.}} A real institution's classifier
misclassification, robust across bootstrap resampling (86\%+ wrongly
predicted regardless of training composition), was traced to a real,
independent governance cause: its accreditor placed it on show-cause
status and withdrew accreditation, the actual proximate cause of its
eventual closure, not any financial metric. Tested first as an
additional statistically-fit input alongside the continuous features
already in use --- exactly the treatment this definition says not to
apply --- and it produced a real improvement on the specific institution
but a new, different misclassification elsewhere in the panel,
consistent with what fitting a weight against only a handful of positive
cases would be expected to do: shift the boundary, rather than add
clean, independent information. Retested as a direct override per this
definition's own terms --- bypassing the statistical classifier entirely
wherever the attestation is present --- and it resolved cleanly, with no
new misclassification introduced elsewhere. This is a genuine, if
small-sample, empirical confirmation of the distinction this definition
draws on theoretical grounds alone: a rare, real, external verdict
behaves differently, and should be used differently, than a continuous
proxy competing for statistical weight.

\emph{\textbf{A conceptual note on what P\_o and P\_p actually are, not
yet tested against data.}} The following reframes Definition 6.1 in
plainer terms and draws one further definition from it; nothing here has
been validated the way the results above were, and it should be read as
an interpretive aid and a candidate hypothesis, not an additional
confirmed finding. A container's actual boundary --- the real extent of
what P\_p, the operational representation, is tracking --- is not fixed
by any label, charter, or org chart. It is constituted by wherever actor
energy is actually, currently interfacing with a recognizable pattern:
employees showing up, money changing hands, a customer returning, a
reader engaging a text. Call that recognizable pattern the container's
\emph{energetic signature}. P\_o, the official representation, is a
claim about that signature --- a name, a charter, a legal boundary, a
board's attestation --- but the claim and the thing it claims to
describe are not the same object, and D\_op (§6.1's divergence measure)
is precisely the gap between them. Ordinary institutional decline, of
the kind this document's adapters were built to track, is the signature
drifting from the container's real activity gradually enough that the
gap eventually surfaces in the container's own downstream numbers ---
which is exactly why an effect-layer adapter, watching only aggregate
financial and enrollment outcomes, can still detect it without ever
observing the underlying actors directly.

A sharper case is worth naming on its own: deliberate fraud, in this
vocabulary, is the manufacture of a signature specifically engineered to
have no real container behind it, built so that an outside observer
mistakes the claim for the fact it purports to describe. A fabricated
governing board --- one an investigation later found to include members
who did not exist and others who had never heard of the organization ---
is a clean, real-world instance: not an institution whose official and
operational layers had drifted apart over time, but a signature
constructed from the outset to simulate independent oversight where no
corresponding actor-energy (people actually reviewing the organization's
spending) was ever present at all. This suggests a real, testable
asymmetry worth flagging for any future adapter attempting to detect it:
ordinary drift tends to leak into a container's own real-world numbers
eventually, which is what allows an effect-layer tracker to catch it
without direct access to actors; a signature engineered to fake its own
container is built specifically to suppress that leak, which is a
plausible reason mechanism-layer evidence --- the kind an effect-layer
adapter cannot supply --- becomes necessary rather than merely
convenient once fraud, rather than ordinary institutional decline, is
the actual phenomenon under investigation. Stated here as a hypothesis
to test against real cases, not as something this project's data has yet
confirmed.

\textbf{Metamessenger Composition Variables}

The extraction and convergence equations of the core engine use four
composition variables of the meta-representation layer, stated here: the
upward transmission fraction ω\_meta\^{}up(t) (Definition 10.5b.18.2),
the closure-orientation fraction Ψ\_loyal(t) (Definition 10.5b.18.12),
the effective operational mass μ\_p\^{}eff(t) (Definition 10.5b.18.13),
and the total convergence-favorability Ψ\_total(t) (Definition
10.5b.18.14). The meta-representation layer itself is not an extension
point: it is a core primitive (Definition 6.1), and its distribution
P\_m enters the feedback potential of §6.4 directly, so that the
three-channel potential requires it; these four variables are summary
refinements of that layer's composition, and their inactive
configuration is stated in Definition 10.5b.18.14a. Their
regime-specific mechanisms (captive official layers, the firewall's
operation, and the corroborated refinement of Ψ\_loyal) are in the
catalog, §10.5b.18. The downward channel ω\_meta\^{}down(t) (Definition
10.5b.18.5) and the valve symmetry σ\_valve(t) (Definition 10.5b.18.6)
are catalog quantities; where this document mentions them (§14, §15.2),
it refers to their catalog definitions. Where the upward channel
ω\_meta(t) of Definition 10.5b.18.2 is set beside the downward channel,
it is written ω\_meta\^{}up(t).

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.2 (Metamessenger Composition).}}

Let the meta-representation layer be composed of a population of
sub-processes, and define ω\_meta(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the
resource-weighted fraction of that population that is truth-aligned in
the sense of Definition 10.5b.1b.1: its output serves the carrier of the
container's original values (in either self-deceptive regime, by
carrying operational reality upward to the official layer), with
1−ω\_meta(t) comfort-aligned (its output is conditioned on keeping the
official layer's existing model intact, whatever operational reality
is). Both terms name what a sub-process's output is conditioned on, not
a motive; in an immune system the two are effector signaling that
reports the pathogen load and regulatory signaling that holds the
existing tolerance in place, and in a software system they are a
monitoring process that reports faults and a suppression filter that
keeps the service's status page green. ω\_meta(t) is regime-dependent by
construction, not a free parameter:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Low-entropy baseline: ω\_meta(t) ≈ 1 and the population is thin ---
  not functioning as a firewall at all, but as a permeable membrane
  (Proposition 10.5b.1.6) carrying ordinary bidirectional reporting,
  consistent with D\_om's healthy target band (§10.5b.1).
\item
  High-entropy: ω\_meta(t) is typically near ½ (Proposition
  10.5b.1b.2.1), with the comfort-aligned part of the population
  actively protecting the official layer's stability by withholding
  operational reality --- this is the firewall in its most consequential
  form, since the operational reality being withheld is genuinely
  corrupted.
\item
  Medium-entropy: ω\_meta(t) is also typically near ½, structurally the
  same configuration as the high-entropy case, and its comfort-aligned
  part is a mistake specific to this regime: the operational layer here
  is the trustworthy one (§10.5b.1), so a comfort-aligned majority is
  withholding an accurate signal rather than a corrupted one, prolonging
  an unnecessary split.
\end{itemize}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.12 (Closure-Orientation
Composition).}}

Among the comfort-aligned sub-population 1−ω\_meta\^{}up(t) maintaining
upward closure at time t, define Ψ\_loyal(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the fraction
whose closure is conditional --- it lifts once the shock it withholds
against has arrived, so that it keeps the container from absorbing that
shock before it is positioned to absorb it intact (negentropic
orientation) --- with 1−Ψ\_loyal(t) the fraction whose closure is
unconditional --- it persists through and after the shock, so that it
keeps the entropic layer in place (entropic orientation). Orientation is
defined by what the closure does across a threshold event, not by any
attribution of motive, and is read from that behavior: Definition
10.5b.18.13 states its consequences, and the proxy below reads its
trend. Domain instances. Immune regulatory suppression that withholds an
inflammatory response and lifts once an infection clears is conditional
closure; a tumor's exploitation of the same checkpoint pathway, holding
the response off indefinitely, is unconditional. A circuit breaker that
resets once its fault clears is conditional; one held open past the
fault is unconditional. This is orthogonal to ω\_meta\^{}up(t) and
ω\_meta\^{}down(t) by construction: those measure behavior, whether
transmission occurs; Ψ\_loyal(t) measures orientation, whether the
absence of transmission is conditional, among exactly the population for
whom that question is live. Two containers can present identical
ω\_meta\^{}up(t), ω\_meta\^{}down(t), and σ\_valve(t) readings while
differing sharply in Ψ\_loyal(t), and current behavior alone cannot
distinguish them.

Ψ\_loyal(t) is not directly observable from a single-timepoint reading.
Where the domain adapter has no direct measurement, this document gives
a longitudinal proxy rather than leaving the quantity uncomputable:
negentropic-loyal closure characteristically shows ω\_meta\^{}up(t)
drifting slowly upward even while still below the threshold that would
register as open (the mechanism already described, for the high-entropy
case specifically, in §10.5b.18.2's editorial note on rogue
metamessengers); entropic-loyal closure shows a flat or declining trend
absent external shock. Formally,

Ψ\_loyal(t) ≈ σ(κ\_loyal · Δω\_meta\^{}up(t; w\_loyal)),

where Δω\_meta\^{}up(t; w\_loyal) is the trailing-window slope of
ω\_meta\^{}up over window w\_loyal, σ(·) a logistic squashing function,
and κ\_loyal, w\_loyal fitted. This is a proxy, not a substitute for
direct measurement where the domain supports one (in an institution, an
internal survey or dissent-channel content analysis; in an immune
system, checkpoint-signal expression across the course of an infection;
or any equivalent instrument reading the conditionality of closure
rather than only output); the trend-based default should be treated with
the same caution as every other conservative default in this document
(§15.2) --- it will understate Ψ\_loyal(t) for a genuinely negentropic
population that has not yet begun to shift its behavior at all, and
should not be read as ruling out high loyalty merely because the trend
is still flat.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.13 (Loyalty-Conditioned
Convergence).}}

Ψ\_loyal(t) is not merely descriptive; it conditions how a container's
existing convergence-outcome machinery resolves under a lateral knock,
in both non-baseline regimes. The medium-entropy case is stated here.
The high-entropy case conditions the structural hazard rate Ω\_hazard of
the verticality account (Definition 10.5b.0.4), so it is stated in the
catalog with that account's quantities, §10.5b.18.13.

\emph{Medium-entropy (official-exposed, Flip Condition B).} §10.5b.7
already determines the B1-lock-versus-B2-reorganization split by
fluid-mass weighting, μ\_o against μ\_p, at the moment of launch. Define
an effective operational mass,

μ\_p\^{}eff(t) = μ\_p · (1 + κ\_ML · Ψ\_loyal(t)), κ\_ML \textgreater{}
0 fitted,

used in place of μ\_p in §10.5b.7's B1/B2 mass-weighted split from this
point forward. A high-loyalty closed population (Ψ\_loyal(t) → 1)
releases operational truth once the knock's threshold event begins,
rather than continuing to protect the official story through it, and so
effectively adds its own weight to the operational side of the split,
strengthening B2's momentum. Which route is taken is not set by this
mass comparison: the direction is set by Definition 10.5b.1b.6, in which
the same loyal members cross to the truth-aligned side during the knock.
A low-loyalty population (Ψ\_loyal(t) → 0) leaves μ\_p\^{}eff(t) ≈ μ\_p
unchanged, continuing to protect the official layer straight through the
knock, which is the B1-lock behavior this document already treats as the
default case.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.14 (Total Convergence-Favorability).}}

Define Ψ\_total(t) = ω\_meta\^{}up(t) + (1 −
ω\_meta\^{}up(t))·Ψ\_loyal(t) --- the fraction of the \emph{entire}
metamessenger population, not only the closed sub-population Ψ\_loyal(t)
is defined over (§10.5b.18.12), that is negentropic in either behavior
or orientation: already-transmitting (ω\_meta\^{}up(t)) plus
closed-but-protectively-oriented ((1 − ω\_meta\^{}up(t))·Ψ\_loyal(t)).
This requires no new adapter input; it is computed entirely from
quantities this document already defines. Ψ\_total(t) is the operational
layer's own estimate --- to whatever extent the operational layer can be
said to have access to this composition information, which in a
medium-entropy container with full systems access is generally the case
--- of how a sudden permeability event would resolve if one occurred
now: a high Ψ\_total(t) predicts convergence toward operational reality
(D\_pm → 0) under a knock; a low one predicts convergence toward
official (D\_om → 0).

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.14a (Inactive Configuration of the
Composition Variables).}}

\emph{The composition variables are not additive terms, so their
inactive state is not zero but a neutral configuration at which they
drop out of the core equations: ω\_meta\^{}up(t) = 1 and Ψ\_loyal(t) =
0, which give μ\_p\^{}eff(t) = μ\_p and Ψ\_total(t) = 1, so that the
B1/B2 split of §10.5b.7, the resilience accumulator, and every other
quantity using these variables reduce to their forms without the
composition mechanisms. Convention: Ψ\_loyal(t) := 0 whenever
ω\_meta\^{}up(t) = 1, since the closed sub-population over which
Ψ\_loyal is defined (Definition 10.5b.18.12) is then empty and has no
orientation to read; the trend proxy is evaluated only where 1 −
ω\_meta\^{}up(t) \textgreater{} 0. This makes μ\_p\^{}eff and Ψ\_total
continuous at full transmission whatever the proxy would otherwise
return.}

\emph{Two conditions are distinguished and are not to be conflated. A
composition mechanism that is not in use is fixed at this neutral
configuration, as a modeling choice stated by the adapter. A composition
variable that is in use but unmeasured takes the default or proxy of
§15.2, subject to that section's precedence rules (regime indeterminacy
checked first; the default of 1 withheld for a container already
classified as non-baseline), and is reported as defaulted rather than
measured. The two can coincide numerically --- the §15.2 default for
ω\_meta\^{}up is also 1 --- without being the same condition: only the
first asserts that the mechanism is absent, and only the second carries
a defaulted-input flag into every quantity computed from it.}

\hypertarget{representation-consistency}{%
\subsection{6.2 Representation
Consistency}\label{representation-consistency}}

If every representation accurately reflects the underlying information
state, then P\_o = P\_p = P\_m = P --- maximal informational integrity.
More generally, representation error is unavoidable; adaptive systems
continuously approximate, rather than perfectly reproduce, their own
informational organization.

\hypertarget{informational-divergence}{%
\subsection{6.3 Informational
Divergence}\label{informational-divergence}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 6.2 (Pairwise Divergence).}}

For any two representations P\_i, P\_j, define D\_ij = D(P\_i, P\_j),
where D is an admissible informational distance from Definition 4.2 (any
of the six listed functionals is valid here --- this is a scalar use,
not a Riemannian one; see §4.2). The pairwise divergences become D\_op,
D\_om, D\_pm. Notation: the subscripts o, p and m always name one
container's own three layers, so D\_op, D\_om and D\_pm are divergences
within a single container, written D\_op(i,t) where several containers
appear; a divergence between two containers i and j uses container
indices, D\_ij = D(P\_i, P\_j).

\emph{\textbf{Messaging Divergence (restored naming).}} The collective
term for D\_op, D\_om, and D\_pm together is Messaging Divergence ---
the real, original name for this complex from the framework's earliest
development, before a mid-development generalization (moving from a
single concrete formula to the present Jensen-Shannon/Fisher-Rao
information-geometric treatment) replaced the name with its bare
component symbols without carrying the name itself forward as a named
successor. Restored here because the earlier loss was of naming and
explanatory continuity specifically, not of any mathematical capability:
every quantity the original name described is still computed exactly as
before, under D\_op/D\_om/D\_pm; only the umbrella term connecting them
to what they collectively represent --- divergence in what is actually
being communicated across a container's own three layers --- had gone
missing. Messaging Divergence is not a new quantity; it is D\_op + D\_om
+ D\_pm (or, where a single scalar summary is wanted, the same
combination already used as Φ\_t's base potential, §6.4), named plainly.

These quantities measure different informational failures.
Official-versus-operational divergence may reflect implementation
failure, whereas operational-versus-meta divergence reflects inaccurate
internal self-monitoring.

\textbf{Computational instantiation (concrete default for D).}

For discrete or discretized Ω --- the normal case for real data --- take
D as symmetrized, additively smoothed Jensen--Shannon divergence:

\emph{D}(P\_i, P\_j) = ½·KL(P\_i ‖ M) + ½·KL(P\_j ‖ M), where M = ½(P\_i
+ P\_j), using base-2 logarithms throughout, so that D is normalized to
{[}0,1{]} rather than {[}0, ln 2{]} --- stated explicitly because every
later threshold and asymptotic statement in this document that
references D approaching 1 (D\_op → 1, and similar) presumes the unit
interval, and the maximum of Jensen--Shannon divergence otherwise
depends on which logarithm base is used. An implementation using natural
logarithms throughout will obtain the same qualitative dynamics but a
different numerical ceiling (ln 2 ≈ 0.693 rather than 1), and should
rescale accordingly rather than apply this document's stated thresholds
unmodified against a differently-normalized D.

with each empirical distribution first smoothed as P̂(x) = (count(x) + ε)
/ (N + εΩ) for small ε \textgreater{} 0 (Laplace smoothing), which keeps
every KL term finite even when a category is unobserved in one
representation but not the other --- a routine occurrence in sparse
real-world data and a hard failure mode for unsmoothed KL. This single
default replaces the six-option menu of §4.2 for every scalar
(non-Riemannian) use of D in Parts 6, 7, and 11; an application may
substitute Wasserstein or another admissible distance from §4.2 if its Ω
is naturally continuous or ordinal, but JS-with-smoothing is the default
assumed by every other formula in this Part unless stated otherwise.

\hypertarget{convergence-does-not-require-unimodality}{%
\subsection{6.3.1 Convergence Does Not Require
Unimodality}\label{convergence-does-not-require-unimodality}}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 6.3.1 (Plural Convergence).}}

Let P\_p genuinely hold substantial mass on two or more distinct,
non-adjacent regions of Ω --- a container's operational reality can be
legitimately multi-valent, not merely uncertain between adjacent
possibilities, where the underlying situation itself admits more than
one simultaneously true characterization. D(P\_m, P\_p), computed per
§6.3's Jensen--Shannon instantiation, depends only on how well P\_m's
distribution matches P\_p's, mode for mode and mass for mass; it does
not penalize P\_p or P\_m for being multi-modal, and two multi-modal
distributions that match each other closely produce as low a D\_pm as
two single-mode distributions that match each other closely.
Convergence, so measured, is agreement between representations, not
agreement on a single outcome.

\emph{\textbf{Corollary 6.3.1.1 (Forced Flattening Increases
Divergence).}}

Where P\_m is constrained --- by design, by adapter assumption, or by
the meta-representation layer's own composition --- to resolve toward a
single dominant mode regardless of P\_p's actual shape, and P\_p is
genuinely multi-modal, that constraint does not produce convergence; it
produces a new source of divergence, since D(P\_m, P\_p) between a
flattened P\_m and a genuinely plural P\_p is higher than D(P\_m, P\_p)
would be between two matched plural distributions. A meta-representation
layer that resolves internal contradiction by picking one true thing
about the operational reality and discarding the other is not more
converged than one that holds both; it is measurably less converged, by
the same divergence measure this document already uses everywhere else.

\hypertarget{grounding-a-second-axis-distinct-from-internal-agreement}{%
\subsection{6.3.2 Grounding: A Second Axis Distinct From Internal
Agreement}\label{grounding-a-second-axis-distinct-from-internal-agreement}}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} D\_op(t), D\_om(t), and D\_pm(t) measure
agreement among a container's own representations --- official,
operational, meta --- entirely internally. Low divergence on these
measures has, throughout this document, been read as a signature of
health. But internal agreement and accuracy are not the same property,
and a container's own representations, however closely they agree with
each other, supply no way to distinguish the two from inside the
container itself: three mutually-reinforcing representations that have
independently converged on the same inaccurate account of the
container's own situation produce exactly the same low D\_op, D\_om,
D\_pm readings a genuinely healthy, accurate container would produce.
None of the three internal measures can tell these apart.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 6.3.2 (Grounding).}}

Define G(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, a container's grounding: how well P(t) tracks a
reality independent of any representation the container itself
generated, as opposed to how well the container's own representations
agree with one another. Where an adapter can supply access to a
genuinely independent external party k, uncaptured by the container
under assessment and possessing real access to its true state ---
κ\_access(k, C, t) ≈ 1, per §10.5b.18.9.1's observational-depth
machinery, reused here rather than duplicated --- grounding is read
directly from that party's own accuracy-weighted assessment:

G(t) = A\_acc(k, C, t) · {[}1 − D(P(t), P\_k(t)){]},

G(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} requires D normalized to {[}0,1{]} and A\_acc ∈
{[}0,1{]}; the base-2 Jensen--Shannon default satisfies the first, and
an adapter using an unbounded functional, such as Wasserstein distance,
normalizes it before use. The same holds for the grounding term Γ\_t
(Definition 6.4a).

where P\_k(t) is k's own independent representation of C's state. Where
no such external party is available, G(t) is undefined rather than
assumed --- there is no introspective substitute for it, and a container
reporting high internal confidence about its own grounding is not
evidence of grounding, since that confidence is generated by the same
internal apparatus whose accuracy is in question.

\emph{\textbf{Why D\_op(t) low does not imply G(t) high.}} D\_op, D\_om,
and D\_pm are computed entirely from P\_o, P\_p, and P\_m --- three
representations all ultimately traceable to the container's own P and
its own meta-representation population. If P itself has drifted from the
reality it is supposed to track, and all three representations have
drifted together, internal agreement is preserved exactly as fully as it
would be under genuine accuracy. A container can be perfectly,
measurably converged by every internal diagnostic this document provides
and simultaneously be almost entirely wrong about the reality it
inhabits. G(t) is the only quantity in this document that can register
that difference, and it is definitionally unavailable from inside the
container producing the readings being checked.

\emph{\textbf{Computed estimate versus true grounding.}} The formula
above produces Ĝ(t), a computed grounding estimate, not G(t) itself.
True grounding is latent unless the conditions the formula assumes ---
that k is genuinely independent, genuinely uncaptured, and genuinely
informed --- are themselves established, and those conditions are not
automatically verifiable from within the assessment. Two observers who
appear independent but in fact both inherit the same upstream
misinformation will agree with each other and with the container being
assessed, producing a high Ĝ(t) with no genuine external validation
behind it; this is not a contradiction in the formula, it is a real
identifiability limit on what any computed grounding estimate can
guarantee. Ĝ(t) should be read accordingly: a genuine ceiling on
confidence when k's independence is itself well-established (e.g.,
adapter-confirmed structural separation from the assessed container's
own history and coupling network), and a much weaker signal otherwise
--- high Ĝ(t) computed against a k whose own independence is merely
assumed does not license the same confidence as high Ĝ(t) computed
against a k whose independence is actually confirmed.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.9.1 (Feedback Accuracy).}}

External feedback's capacity to help a container heal depends on
accuracy against the target's actual true state, not on the source's
sincerity: a source that has never had access to a target's true state
cannot supply accurate feedback about it, however honestly delivered.
Define the accuracy ceiling A\_acc(source, i, t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} for
feedback about container i's true state, in two cases.

Self-report (source = i, i reporting on its own state, as in
§10.5b.18.9's φ\_fb):

A\_acc(i, i, t) = 1 − D\_om(i,t)

--- i's own accuracy about itself is bounded by its own
meta-representation's existing divergence from its own operational
reality; a high-entropy container's self-report is structurally
inaccurate by the same divergence that already characterizes its regime,
independent of how sincerely it believes what it reports. This requires
no new quantity: D\_om(i,t) already exists (§6.3).

Third-party report (source j ≠ i, j reporting on i's state): define
κ\_access(j,i,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, j's observational depth into i ---
adapter-supplied where a direct measure of access exists (a documented
insider position, verified transparency, whistleblower-adjacent access),
defaulting to 0 (official-layer-only access) where not otherwise
supplied, consistent with this document's conservative-default
convention (§15.2). Then:

A\_acc(j, i, t) = κ\_access(j,i,t) + (1 − κ\_access(j,i,t))·(1 −
D\_op(i,t))

Full access (κ\_access → 1) allows accuracy approaching 1 regardless of
i's own internal divergence. Official-layer-only access (κ\_access = 0)
caps accuracy at (1 − D\_op(i,t)) --- exactly as good as i's own
official layer tracks i's own truth, and no better, regardless of j's
own sincerity or care in reporting what it saw. This formalizes
directly: if a source does not know a target's true internal state, its
feedback cannot be accurate, no matter how honestly delivered.

\hypertarget{two-channel-reduced-mode}{%
\subsection{6.3.3 Two-Channel Reduced
Mode}\label{two-channel-reduced-mode}}

\textbf{Definition 6.3.3 (Meta-Channel Availability and the Two-Channel
Read-Out).} For a container at time t, let ι\_m(t) ∈ \{0, 1\} be the
meta-channel availability indicator: ι\_m(t) = 1 where P\_m(t) is
supplied by route (i) or route (ii) of §6.4 (directly observed, or
latent but estimated under a stated model), and ι\_m(t) = 0 where it is
supplied by route (iii) (unavailable). ι\_m is a computational
availability indicator: it records whether the engine has the meta
channel as an input at t, not whether the container\textquotesingle s
meta-representation layer exists or functions. A container in
two-channel mode still has a meta layer; the engine simply has no
reading of it. Where the layers are read along several claim dimensions,
ι\_m(t) = 1 exactly when at least one available dimension has a meta
reading; D\_om and D\_pm are then taken over those dimensions, and the
dimensions without a meta reading are in two-channel status (Definitions
6.3.4a and 6.3.4b). The availability-conditioned Messaging Divergence is

D\_msg(t) = D\_op(t) + D\_om(t) + D\_pm(t) if ι\_m(t) = 1; D\_msg(t) =
D\_op(t) if ι\_m(t) = 0,

and, where a single scalar summary is taken as a maximum over channels,
its availability-conditioned form is

D\_max(t) = max\{ D\_op(t), D\_om(t), D\_pm(t) \} if ι\_m(t) = 1;
D\_max(t) = D\_op(t) if ι\_m(t) = 0.

With ι\_m(t) = 1 both reduce to the three-channel quantities of §6.3
unchanged. With ι\_m(t) = 0 both are D\_op(t) by definition, and
D\_om(t) and D\_pm(t) are never evaluated, since one of their arguments
is unavailable; this is the two-channel read-out. (The piecewise form is
deliberate: writing D\_op + ι\_m · (D\_om + D\_pm) would multiply an
undefined quantity by zero, which does not make it defined.) A container
whose meta channel is unavailable is said to be in two-channel mode at
t, and every quantity computed from D\_msg or D\_max at that step
carries the label two-channel. This label is distinct from the
defaulted-input flag of §15.2: a default asserts a value for an
unmeasured variable, whereas two-channel mode asserts no value for P\_m
at all.

\textbf{Definition 6.3.3a (Relaxed Triangle Constant).} An informational
distance D of Definition 4.2 has relaxed triangle constant ρ\_Δ ≥ 1 if,
for all P, Q, Q′ in 𝒫(Ω),

D(P, Q) ≤ ρ\_Δ · {[} D(P, Q′) + D(Q′, Q) {]}.

A true metric has ρ\_Δ = 1: Fisher--Rao geodesic distance, Hellinger
distance, total variation distance and Wasserstein distance (where
admissible) all qualify. Jensen--Shannon divergence has ρ\_Δ = 2,
because its square root is a metric, so that JS(P, Q) ≤ (√JS(P, Q′) +
√JS(Q′, Q))² ≤ 2 · {[}JS(P, Q′) + JS(Q′, Q){]}. Symmetrized
Kullback--Leibler divergence has no finite ρ\_Δ in general. Let D̄ = sup
D(P, Q) over 𝒫(Ω), the diameter of 𝒫(Ω) under D. It is finite for
Jensen--Shannon divergence (log 2), total variation and Hellinger
distance (finite under any of the usual normalizations, the exact value
depending on the normalization), and Fisher--Rao distance (π under the
normalization d\_FR(P, Q) = 2 arccos Σ\_ω √(P(ω) Q(ω)); a different
normalization of Definition 4.1\textquotesingle s Fisher metric scales D̄
accordingly). Wasserstein distance has finite D̄ exactly when the ground
metric on Ω has finite diameter, and then D̄ equals that diameter
(attained by two point masses); on an unbounded ground space D̄ is
infinite. Symmetrized Kullback--Leibler divergence has infinite D̄.

\emph{Symmetry.} Definition 4.2 does not itself require D(P, Q) = D(Q,
P), but every functional it lists is symmetric in the form Part 6 uses
(Kullback--Leibler only in its symmetrized form, §4.2), and the results
below assume it: D is symmetric, so that D\_pm = D(O\_p(P), P\_m) =
D(P\_m, O\_p(P)). An application choosing an asymmetric functional must
restate Propositions 6.3.3b and 6.3.3c for it.

\textbf{Proposition 6.3.3b (What the two-channel read-out determines).}
Let D be symmetric, with relaxed triangle constant ρ\_Δ \textless{} ∞,
and let P\_m be unknown. Then for every admissible P\_m,

(1 + 1/ρ\_Δ) · D\_op ≤ D\_op + D\_om + D\_pm ≤ D\_op + 2 D̄,

D\_op ≤ max\{ D\_op, D\_om, D\_pm \} ≤ max\{ D\_op, D̄ \} = D̄.

For a metric (ρ\_Δ = 1) the lower bound 2 · D\_op is attained, at P\_m =
O\_o(P) or P\_m = O\_p(P). Hence the two-channel read-out D\_op
determines the three-channel Messaging Divergence only up to the
interval {[}(1 + 1/ρ\_Δ) · D\_op, D\_op + 2 D̄{]}, and its maximum
summary only up to {[}D\_op, D̄{]}. The two-channel read-out constrains
the three-channel Messaging Divergence to the stated interval but does
not identify its value within that interval.

\emph{Proof.} Applying Definition 6.3.3a with Q′ = P\_m gives D\_op ≤
ρ\_Δ · (D\_om + D\_pm), so D\_om + D\_pm ≥ D\_op / ρ\_Δ, which is the
lower bound on the sum. The upper bound uses D\_om, D\_pm ≤ D̄. For the
maximum, max\{D\_op, D\_om, D\_pm\} ≥ D\_op trivially and ≤ D̄ since
every term is at most D̄ (D\_op ≤ D̄ included). At P\_m = O\_o(P), D\_om =
0 and D\_pm = D(O\_p(P), O\_o(P)) = D\_op by the symmetry of the
admissible functionals in use, so the sum is 2 · D\_op; likewise at P\_m
= O\_p(P). ∎

Where ρ\_Δ is infinite (symmetrized Kullback--Leibler), Proposition
6.3.3b gives only the trivial bound D\_op ≤ D\_op + D\_om + D\_pm, and
an adapter using that functional should report two-channel quantities as
D\_op alone, without an interval.

\textbf{Proposition 6.3.3c (The reduced potential and its zero set).} In
two-channel mode the divergence potential of §6.4 is replaced by its
reduced form

Φ\_t\^{}(2)(P) = D(O\_o(P), O\_p(P)),

and the grounded potential Φ\_t\^{}G of Definition 6.4a by
Φ\_t\^{}(G,2)(P) = Φ\_t\^{}(2)(P) + Γ\_t(P). The other terms of
§6.4\textquotesingle s flow are retained unchanged insofar as they do
not depend on the unavailable P\_m. Checked term by term: the grounding
term Γ\_t uses the accuracy A\_acc(k, C, t) of external parties k ≠ C,
which by the third-party case of Definition 10.5b.18.9.1 (§6.3.2)
depends on D\_op and κ\_access only, not on P\_m; the default feedback
permeability Π\_f (Definition 6.4) is built from correction-and-response
data and does not involve P\_m; and the history-dependent metric
g\_\{C,t\} (§4.10) does not involve P\_m. The one exception is the
forcing sum F\_total(t): any forcing component that requires P\_m, D\_om
or D\_pm is unavailable at that step and is omitted from the reduced
flow, and the omission is recorded among the step\textquotesingle s
two-channel labels. Omission is not a claim that the component is zero,
which would be a placeholder (Proposition 6.3.3d). The resulting
two-channel flow is the engine\textquotesingle s reduced computation
under incomplete meta-channel information; it is not asserted to
reproduce the full three-channel trajectory. The zero set of
Φ\_t\^{}(2), Z\_Φ\^{}(2) = \{ P : O\_o(P) = O\_p(P) \} contains the zero
set Z\_Φ\^{}(3) = \{ P : O\_o(P) = O\_p(P) = P\_m \} of the
three-channel potential for every P\_m. Consequently Φ\_t\^{}(2)(P) = 0
is necessary for the low-entropy baseline and, whenever the state space
contains an admissible state at which the official and operational
layers agree together with a distinct admissible meta state (the
condition in the proof below), not sufficient: a container with official
and operational layers in agreement may still have a meta layer far from
both, which is the configuration of a meta layer functioning as a
firewall rather than a permeable membrane (§6.1), and two-channel mode
cannot distinguish it from baseline.

\emph{Proof.} If O\_o(P) = O\_p(P) = P\_m then O\_o(P) = O\_p(P), so
Z\_Φ\^{}(3) ⊆ Z\_Φ\^{}(2). For strictness, suppose there exist an
admissible state P with O\_o(P) = O\_p(P) = Q and a distinct admissible
meta state P\_m ≠ Q compatible with that state. Then Φ\_t\^{}(2)(P) = 0
while Φ\_t(P; P\_m, M) = 2 · D(Q, P\_m) \textgreater{} 0, by the
identity property of Definition 4.2 together with D(P, Q) = 0 only for P
= Q, as stated in §6.4. ∎

Φ\_t\^{}(2) is a different observable potential, not an estimate of the
three-channel potential: its zero set contains the three-channel
baseline and is strictly larger whenever the existence condition in
Proposition 6.3.3c holds, because information has been removed.
Accordingly, a statement of Part 6 or later that rests on the gradient
or stationary points of the three-channel Φ\_t holds in two-channel mode
only as restated for Φ\_t\^{}(2). In particular, §6.4\textquotesingle s
statement that the low-entropy baseline is Φ\_t\textquotesingle s global
minimum becomes, in two-channel mode, the statement that the global
minimum set of Φ\_t\^{}(2) is Z\_Φ\^{}(2), which contains the baseline
but does not single it out. Each later use of Φ\_t\textquotesingle s
minima or flow carries the two-channel label when it is evaluated with
Φ\_t\^{}(2).

\textbf{Proposition 6.3.3d (A Fixed Placeholder Does Not Establish
Meta-Channel Availability).} Suppose an unavailable P\_m(t) is replaced
by a fixed distribution Q, chosen by the adapter rather than observed or
estimated, and write D\_om\^{}Q = D(O\_o(P), Q), D\_pm\^{}Q = D(O\_p(P),
Q), D\_msg\^{}Q = D\_op + D\_om\^{}Q + D\_pm\^{}Q, and D\_max\^{}Q =
max\{ D\_op, D\_om\^{}Q, D\_pm\^{}Q \}. Then: (a) Q satisfies neither
route (i) nor route (ii) of §6.4, so ι\_m(t) = 0 and the container is in
two-channel mode whatever value Q takes; (b) D\_om\^{}Q, D\_pm\^{}Q and
D\_msg\^{}Q are fixed functions of (O\_o(P), O\_p(P), Q) alone, so that,
for any joint law of the channels, they carry no information about P\_m
beyond what O\_o(P) and O\_p(P) already carry: I(D\_msg\^{}Q ; P\_m
\textbar{} O\_o(P), O\_p(P)) = 0; (c) D\_msg\^{}Q is the counterfactual
three-channel Messaging Divergence obtained by substituting the
hypothetical meta state P\_m = Q; where D is symmetric with finite
relaxed triangle constant, it lies within the interval of Proposition
6.3.3b, at a point selected by the choice of Q rather than by any data,
and where no finite bound exists it remains that counterfactual value
but no finite interval is asserted; and (d) the same holds for
D\_max\^{}Q, with the interval {[}D\_op, D̄{]} of Proposition 6.3.3b
where D̄ is finite. Hence D\_om\^{}Q, D\_pm\^{}Q, D\_msg\^{}Q and
D\_max\^{}Q are computable but are not three-channel measurements, and
the engine does not treat them as such.

\emph{Proof.} (a) A fixed Q is neither an observation of P\_m(t) nor the
output of a stated state-space model for it, which are the only two ways
§6.4 allows P\_m(t) to be available; by Definition 6.3.3, ι\_m(t) = 0.
(b) Given O\_o(P), O\_p(P) and the constant Q, each of D\_om\^{}Q,
D\_pm\^{}Q and D\_msg\^{}Q is determined, so it is conditionally
constant and its conditional mutual information with any further
variable, P\_m included, is zero. (c) is the definition of D\_msg
evaluated at P\_m = Q; Q is an admissible meta state, so, under the
hypotheses of Proposition 6.3.3b, that proposition places the value in
its interval, and different choices of Q can select different points of
that interval while leaving the observed channel data unchanged. (d)
follows in the same way from the definition of D\_max\^{}Q. ∎

\emph{Remark (what this does and does not say).} Proposition 6.3.3d does
not claim that a placeholder has zero error. Measured against the
container\textquotesingle s actual meta state, as §6.1 defines the
channels\textquotesingle{} measurement errors, the
placeholder\textquotesingle s error ε\_m is the discrepancy between the
supplied Q and the actual P\_m(t), which is in general nonzero and need
not have zero covariance, so the covariance-rank condition of §6.1 is
not the ground of the exclusion. The ground is (a) to (c): a placeholder
represents missing information as if it were measured, and the value it
produces reflects the choice of Q. The scalar instantiation shows how
strongly that choice can act. With channels x\_o, x\_p ∈ {[}0, 1{]} and
D\_ij = \textbar x\_i − x\_j\textbar, a placeholder q inside {[}0, 1{]}
gives a maximum summary that depends on where q sits relative to x\_o
and x\_p; and a value outside {[}0, 1{]}, which is not an admissible
index at all, gives max\{\textbar x\_o − x\_p\textbar, \textbar x\_o −
q\textbar, \textbar x\_p − q\textbar\} = q − min\{x\_o, x\_p\} for q ≥ 1
and max\{x\_o, x\_p\} − q for q ≤ 0, since \textbar x\_o −
x\_p\textbar{} ≤ q − min\{x\_o, x\_p\} in the first case and
\textbar x\_o − x\_p\textbar{} ≤ max\{x\_o, x\_p\} − q in the second. In
either out-of-range case the summary tracks the level of one channel,
not the gap between the official and operational channels, and D\_op
never determines it.

\textbf{Computational instantiation.} For a domain adapter whose three
channels are scalar indices in {[}0, 1{]} with D\_ij = \textbar x\_i −
x\_j\textbar, ρ\_Δ = 1 and D̄ = 1, so in two-channel mode D\_msg = D\_max
= \textbar x\_o − x\_p\textbar, with the three-channel sum known to lie
in {[}2 · \textbar x\_o − x\_p\textbar, \textbar x\_o − x\_p\textbar{} +
2{]} and the three-channel maximum in {[}\textbar x\_o − x\_p\textbar,
1{]}. Every derived quantity that enters the engine only through D\_msg
or D\_max (for example, a general divergence cost proportional to D) is
computed from the two-channel value and labeled two-channel, and its
interpretation is conditional on that label: it measures
official--operational divergence only, and it is not an estimate of the
corresponding three-channel quantity. Writing D\_msg\^{}(3) and
D\_max\^{}(3) for the three-channel values, unobserved in two-channel
mode, the two-channel values D\_msg = D\_op and D\_max = D\_op are lower
bounds on them, with the stronger bound D\_msg\^{}(3) ≥ 2 · D\_op in
this metric instantiation. A downstream quantity that is a monotone
nondecreasing function of either summary inherits the corresponding
lower bound; for other derived quantities no general lower-bound
relationship is asserted. Every quantity that uses D\_om or D\_pm
individually, or P\_m itself (accuracy-based alignment A\_acc = 1 −
D\_om, the composition variables of (catalog) §10.5b.18, and any regime
classification that requires the meta channel), remains indeterminate at
that step, as §6.4\textquotesingle s route (iii) already states, and is
reported as unavailable rather than computed.

\hypertarget{claim-dimensions}{%
\subsection{6.3.4 Claim Dimensions}\label{claim-dimensions}}

Definition 6.1 makes the official and operational representations two
deterministic projections of the same underlying state, and Definition
6.2 compares them by a single divergence D\_op. Every domain's official
