or begins with a departing host\textquotesingle s downward pull (𝟙\_host
= 1). The basis: a high-entropy container tips by losing couplings,
through a decoupling, a resolution kick, or the loss of all its
couplings at once from a single cause, which acts as one large knock;
and a container extracting from a host does not tip while the host
anchors it, so that when the host goes, the launch begins with the
container lurching toward where the host was. Whether simultaneous
decouplings from one cause always act as a single push is a geometric
question this hypothesis leaves to data.

\textbf{Corollary 10.5b.9.2.3 (Only B2 can reorganize).} Under
Hypothesis 10.5b.9.2.2, (L1) or (L2) fails for A1 and A2, so E\_A1 =
E\_A2 = 0; with Proposition 10.5b.9.2.1, only B2 can be eligible. ∎ That
no ineligible route reaches full reorganization at any momentum follows
from the outcome map below, in which the launch channel is closed to it.

\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9.2.4 (Outcome Map).} Three accumulators run
over the tipping window, each mass-weighted by the converging pair as in
§10.5b.7 and normalized by M\_tip\^{}ref: the push accumulator M̃\_push
(§10.5b.7\textquotesingle s M̃\_tip, accumulating the net push N\_B), the
lift accumulator M̃\_up (accumulating the upward component of the net
push) and the spin accumulator M̃\_spin (accumulating the net torque
divided by R\_B). Three thresholds compete: lock, when M̃\_push ≥
M\_lock\^{}eff; launch, when M̃\_up·M̃\_spin ≥ M\_c², open only to an
eligible route; and disintegration, when the chaos pressure D\_chaos
reaches Θ\_disintegrate·Λ\_int (§10.5b.9d.3). The outcome is set by
whichever threshold is reached first: lock gives terminal lock, launch
gives full reorganization, disintegration gives fracture (§§10.5b.9d.3
and 10.5b.9g). If none is reached before the window closes, the
container self-arrests. Exact ties, two thresholds reached at the same
instant, form a set of measure zero in the continuous parameters and are
excluded from the ordinary map; one that occurs is reported as
indeterminate, as at ω\_L = ω* (§10.5b.1b.6). With that convention,
every route and momentum pair receives exactly one outcome. The launch
threshold is a product, mirroring Proposition 10.5b.9.1.4.

Lock depth is defined only when the lock threshold has been reached, as
d\_lock = M̃\_push − M\_lock\^{}eff ≥ 0 at the window\textquotesingle s
close; where launch or disintegration comes first, or no threshold is
reached, there is no lock and no lock depth, and Definition 10.5b.9.2.5
does not apply. It has no upper limit: momentum carried into a false or
corrupted configuration deepens the lock rather than escaping it. {[}The
map is structural given the three accumulators; the product form of the
launch threshold is model-derived from Proposition 10.5b.9.1.4.{]}

\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9.2.5 (A Harder Fall Makes a Deeper Lock).} The
proposed physical relationship is that momentum past the lock threshold
does two things at once: it shortens the fall, leaving less time for an
anchor to catch the container partway down, and it deepens the lock. The
selected models for these are an inverse fall time, τ\_fall =
τ₀·M\_lock\^{}eff / M̃\_push, with τ₀ \textgreater{} 0 fitted in units of
time (the ratio is dimensionless), and landing damage linear in depth,
ΔδR\_land = κ\_land·d\_lock, with κ\_land in resource-debt units per
unit of normalized depth, with the anchoring needed to quiet the lock
(§10.5b.9) and the external redirection needed to open a route to
reorganization (§10.5b.11a) both scaling with d\_lock. {[}The direction
of each effect is the proposed relationship; the inverse and linear
forms are chosen models, to be tested.{]} Two everyday instances. A
bamboo bowl that slams down upside down ends in the same position as one
that tipped over gently, but it is wedged in place and harder to pick
up. A suction cup locks tighter the harder and faster it is pressed: the
press drives out the air beneath it, and the more air is driven out, the
larger the pressure difference holding it down and the harder it is to
pull off. The landing\textquotesingle s force drives out what might have
kept the layers apart, and the more it drives out, the more it takes to
break the lock.

\textbf{Remark 10.5b.9.2.6 (Why a false pair falls hard).} On a route
converging on the false layer, the converging pair is the official and
meta layers, carrying mass share q = (μ\_o + μ\_m) / (μ\_o + μ\_p +
μ\_m), about two thirds when the layers are comparable in mass: most of
the container\textquotesingle s mass moves toward the false
configuration at the lip of the tip, so it falls rather than rising.

\hypertarget{b.9a-backslide-dynamics-and-the-resilience-accumulator-zt}{%
\subsection{10.5b.9a Backslide Dynamics and the Resilience Accumulator
Z(t)}\label{b.9a-backslide-dynamics-and-the-resilience-accumulator-zt}}

A container moving toward recovery, whether the high-entropy container
under sustained anchoring (§10.5b.10) or the medium-entropy container
progressing toward B2, does not improve monotonically. Real containers
backslide --- temporarily returning toward elevated σ\_o --- and the
character of those backslides differs sharply depending on which
convergence direction the container is actually moving in, not merely
which regime it currently occupies.

\textbf{Three backslide signatures.}

There are three distinct cases. Duration and cost move together, both
tracking resilience Z directly rather than sharing one single direction
across all three: duration shortens and cost falls as Z rises (the two
self-healing and healing directions), while duration lengthens and cost
rises as Z falls (the collapsing direction) --- a depleted-resilience
system takes both longer and more to get through each successive
episode, not one at the expense of the other. The three cases differ
sharply in valence:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  High-entropy, anchored self-healing (Flip toward D\_pm → 0 under
  sustained anchoring, §10.5b.10): backslide cost, c\_n\^{}(high) =
  ζ·Π\_f(t\_n)·D\_op(t\_n), is a product of rising permeability against
  falling unresolved divergence, and is not guaranteed to move in one
  direction throughout healing --- whether it rises or falls at a given
  point depends on their relative rates of change, not on Π\_f(t) alone.
  The theoretically expected shape is an initial rise as increasing
  permeability exposes divergence faster than confrontation can resolve
  it, followed by an eventual fall once D\_op itself is sufficiently
  reduced --- a real, testable inverted-U pattern, not a monotonic claim
  in either direction. This is the mechanism of learning through direct
  confrontation with consequence, and the early-rising portion in
  particular is what makes that confrontation genuinely costly rather
  than free.
\item
  Medium-entropy healing (D\_pm → 0 convergence direction, heading
  toward B2): each backslide costs less than the last --- resilience
  accumulating through successful recovery from repeated knocks. This is
  the mechanism of learning through resilience.
\item
  Medium-entropy collapsing (D\_om → 0 convergence direction, heading
  toward B1): cost follows the same confrontation mechanism as the
  high-entropy case above --- the same real inverted-U possibility
  applies, now keyed to D\_om rather than D\_op, per §10.5b.3's
  generalized F\_conf, layered underneath the separate, guaranteed
  resilience-depletion penalty (κ\_backslide, Definition 10.5b.9a.2)
  that does rise monotonically as Z falls.
\end{itemize}

The first and third cases share a mechanism (generalized F\_conf) and
differ only in which divergence term is doomed; the second case requires
a genuinely new quantity, since nothing already in this document
produces a cost that shrinks over successive knocks.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9a.1 (Resilience Accumulator).}}

For a medium-entropy container, let s(t) ∈ \{+1,−1\} indicate the
currently-dominant convergence direction (+1 for D\_pm → 0, healing; −1
for D\_om → 0, collapsing). For computational purposes, s(t) is not an
independent state input: it is assigned by the flip-direction rule of
Definition 10.5b.1b.6, from the meta layer's composition at launch ω\_L
against the tipping share ω*, unless directly observed convergence data
are available, in which case the direct observation takes precedence.
Define the resilience accumulator Z(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}:

dZ/dt = s(t) · η\_Z · (Z(t) − ε\_Z)(1 − ε\_Z − Z(t)) · N\_B(t),

with η\_Z \textgreater{} 0 fitted. Z updates only during active
knock/backslide episodes (via Drive\_B, §10.5b.6), not continuously ---
resilience is built or eroded by what a container actually survives, not
by the passage of time alone. The logistic form keeps Z bounded and most
sensitive to change away from its extremes.

\emph{\textbf{Floor and ceiling convention.}} The pure logistic form
above has absorbing boundaries at Z=0 and Z=1: dZ/dt = 0 exactly at
either extreme regardless of s(t), so a container whose resilience is
driven to precisely zero can never recover through this mechanism alone,
however its circumstances later change. This is not intended as a claim
that total exhaustion is permanent. Z is bounded to {[}ε\_Z, 1−ε\_Z{]}
rather than {[}0,1{]}, with a small fitted ε\_Z \textgreater{} 0,
enforced by the rescaled logistic above, whose only fixed points are
ε\_Z and 1 − ε\_Z, so a trajectory starting inside the interval cannot
leave it (discrete implementations clip to the interval after each
step): the logistic dynamics approach but never reach either true
absorbing boundary, so gradual recovery from even severe depletion
remains always technically possible, at whatever rate the container's
circumstances allow, rather than foreclosed outright. This is
independent of, and does not substitute for, the emergency mechanisms of
§10.5b.14.2 or the systems-override mechanism of §10.5b.18.16 --- those
are deliberate interventions; this is a background property of the
resilience quantity itself.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9a.2 (Backslide Cost and Duration).}}

Let t\_n denote the time of the n-th knock. For the medium-entropy cases
(s = ±1), duration follows the same monotonic relationship to resilience
regardless of direction:

τ\_n = τ\_0 · (1 − Z(t\_n)),

so that higher Z always shortens the excursion. Cost follows the same
underlying direction --- higher resilience lowers cost --- in both
cases, stated in the surface language natural to each direction (rising
Z lowering cost for the healing case; falling Z raising cost for the
collapsing case, which is the identical relationship read the other way,
not an opposite one):

\emph{c}\_n = c\_0 · (1 − Z(t\_n)) · {[}1 + κ\_backslide · (1 − Z(t\_n))
· 𝟙(s(t) = −1){]}, κ\_backslide ≥ 0 fitted (the linear base dependence
on Z is a computational default consistent with the theoretical
direction --- cost falls as resilience rises, in both cases --- but is
not itself forced by that direction; alternative curves sharing the same
monotonic relationship remain admissible),

so that for s = +1 (healing), rising Z drives both cost and duration
down, with κ\_backslide contributing nothing; for s = −1 (collapsing),
falling Z drives cost up along the same base relationship, further
amplified by the depletion penalty κ\_backslide · (1 − Z(t\_n))
specifically as resilience runs low, while duration correspondingly
lengthens per τ\_n = τ\_0·(1 − Z(t\_n)) above (the excursion is both
longer and, at low Z particularly, more damaging per unit time than the
healing direction's base relationship alone would give --- a
depleted-resilience trajectory takes both more and longer, not a
shorter, sharper penalty). κ\_backslide = 0 recovers the plain shared
relationship with no directional asymmetry at all, which is a legitimate
modeling choice if no real, measured difference in severity between
healing and collapsing excursions is found. For the high-entropy
self-healing case, cost is given directly by the generalized F\_conf of
§10.5b.3:

\emph{c}\_n\^{}(high) = F\_conf(t\_n) = ζ · Π\_f(t\_n) · D\_op(t\_n).

Resilience also feeds back into the feedback operator's own drift rate
for a medium-entropy healing container, making correction itself more
effective as resilience accumulates:

\emph{γ}\_ℱ(t) = γ\_ℱ,0 · (1 + κ\_Z · Z(t)), κ\_Z \textgreater{} 0
fitted, applied in §6.4's stochastic flow for this container.

10.5b.9a.3 Repeated Full-Cycle Reorganization Without Baseline Reset

A distinct pattern from the backslide dynamics above, operating at a
different timescale: §10.5b.9a's backslides occur within a single
container's approach to one tipping events resolution. This subsection
addresses a container that completes an entire hull-breach-to-B2 cycle,
genuinely resolving that specific tipping event via full reorganization,
without that reorganization sustaining into low-entropy baseline
re-entry --- Proposition 8.8a.3's own "may re-enter... if sustained"
already leaves this failure of sustaining as a live possibility rather
than an edge case, and this subsection formalizes what happens when it
recurs.

Proposition 10.5b.9a.3 (Instant Baseline Reset Is the Exception, Not the
Rule). A medium-entropy containers entropic official layer (D\_om) is
not, in general, corrected merely by a single successful B2
reorganization resolving the operational-layer divergence (D\_pm) that
triggered that specific tipping event: D\_om's own correction is a
separate, slower process (§10.5b.14.1's rogue-metamessenger and
slow-correction machinery), and a container can complete B2 repeatedly
--- genuinely avoiding B1 lock each time, via §10.5b.14.3b's Systems
Override --- while D\_om itself remains only partially or temporarily
corrected between cycles, re-approaching hull-breach again once
sufficient time or renewed pressure passes. Instant, permanent baseline
reset on a container's first B2 is therefore the special case where
D\_om happens to be corrected in the same event that resolves D\_pm, not
the general pattern: a container whose D\_om requires its own sustained,
separate correction over multiple cycles is the more common case, not an
anomaly requiring separate explanation.

Definition 10.5b.9a.3a (Cycle Count and Cumulative Override Debt). Let
n(t) denote the number of complete hull-breach-to-B2 cycles a container
has undergone up to time t without a sustained low-entropy baseline
re-entry between any of them. Each cycle's own Systems Override
intervention (§10.5b.14.3b.ii) carries its own real resource cost
(already established, not newly introduced here); what this definition
adds is that the cost of sustaining override capacity itself ---
operational's own continued readiness to intervene, cycle after cycle,
without the underlying condition resolving --- accrues as a function of
n(t) directly:

M\_cycle(t) = κ\_cycle · n(t), κ\_cycle \textgreater{} 0 fitted,
entering δR(t) alongside this document's other resource-debt
contributions (§7.9's convention).

This is a real, distinct cost from any single cycle's own override
expenditure: a container that has survived ten cycles carries a real,
cumulative burden from having needed ten interventions, independent of
how cheaply any individual intervention was purchased, structurally
parallel to §10.5b.18.23b's escalating suppression cost --- repetition
itself is not free, even when every individual instance of the repeated
event is successfully handled.

Proposition 10.5b.9a.3b (The Cycle Does Not Resolve Itself; D\_om Must
Still Be Addressed). A container sustaining repeated successful B2
cycles is genuinely resilient in the sense that matters most immediately
--- it is not failing, and each individual crisis is being met rather
than lost to B1 lock --- but resilience of this kind is not the same as
resolution, and Z(t)'s own rise (§10.5b.9a.1) from successfully
surviving repeated knocks does not, by itself, correct D\_om. Absent
continued, real progress on D\_om specifically (rather than merely on
surviving each acute episode as it arises), the pattern has exactly two
possible long-run resolutions, not an indefinite stable middle: either
D\_om eventually falls enough for a cycle's own B2 to sustain into
genuine low-entropy baseline re-entry, or operational's own override
capacity, cycle after cycle, eventually proves insufficient against a
D\_om that has not improved, and a future cycle resolves as B1 rather
than B2. Repeated successful cycling buys time and demonstrates real
capability; it does not substitute for the slower, separate work of
correcting the official layer's own entropic distortion, and treating
survival of the cycle as sufficient in itself is the specific, real risk
this proposition names.

10.5b.9a.3c Metamessenger Ratio Drift and the Last Collapse

Definition 10.5b.9a.3c (Directional Drift of Ψ\_loyal Across Cycles).
§10.5b.9a.3's repeated cycling has a real, checkable direction rather
than being an undifferentiated sequence of
equally-likely-to-resolve-either-way events: Ψ\_loyal(t) (§10.5b.18.12,
the fraction of a container's own comfort-aligned metamessenger
population still maintaining closure with the entropic official layer,
versus the operational layer's own patient, repeated attempts to
reorganize it) shifts, however slightly, with every cycle --- inaccurate
feedback from coupled systems or the ambient grid, decoupling of a
supportive system or anchor, ongoing lateral knocks from extractive
systems, or any input that moves the container further toward its own
collapse direction (D\_om → 0, §10.5b.18.17) all push Ψ\_loyal toward
the entropic pole; successful reorganization, genuine support, or
operational's own patient correction push it toward alignment. The
direction this ratio has been moving across a container's recent cycle
history is real, predictive signal for which branch (eventual 360 reset
versus eventual 180 lock) a future decisive cycle is more likely to
resolve toward, though it does not fix the outcome of any single future
cycle with certainty.

Proposition 10.5b.9a.3f (Ambient Push as a Threshold Effect, Not a
Variable-Strength One). The ambient grid's own contribution to
Ψ\_loyal's drift, named among the factors above, is a fixed-magnitude
push, not one whose own strength scales with how close the container's
internal metamessenger population already is to an even split. Its
decisiveness for the eventual outcome is nonetheless a real threshold
effect: a fixed ambient push cannot change which side wins an
already-decisive internal contest --- a population already strongly
skewed toward alignment or toward the entropic pole absorbs the
same-sized push without its eventual resolution direction changing ---
but the identical, unchanged push can flip which side ultimately wins
when the internal margin between the two populations is already narrower
than the push itself. Decisiveness is therefore a property of the
pre-existing internal margin relative to a constant-sized external
quantity, not a claim that the ambient push itself grows stronger as
internal equipoise is approached --- the same
margin-versus-fixed-quantity logic already used for distinguishing
genuine capacity constraint from concealment (§10.5b.18.18k's
τ\_resp-versus-τ\_route test), reapplied here to a
population-composition contest rather than a response-time one. A
container already multiply knocked, with its internal population near an
even split as a direct consequence of that repeated cycling
(§10.5b.9a.3, §10.5b.9a.4c), is for exactly this reason the specific
state in which grid-wide ambient signaling becomes the deciding factor
in its eventual resolution, not because the grid is broadcasting any
harder at that container specifically, but because that container's own
prior history has left it with the narrowest internal margin for an
unchanged ambient push to cross.

Proposition 10.5b.9a.3d (Non-Proportional Shift Magnitude). The
magnitude of a given cycles shift to Ψ\_loyal is not proportional to
that cycle's own apparent severity: Δ Ψ\_loyal(t\_n) ⊉ κ · (event
magnitude), since a comparatively minor event can produce a larger shift
than a major one when the container's existing resource debt δR(t) is
already elevated, or when Ψ\_loyal is already moving decisively in one
direction (a container already trending toward alignment shifts further
toward it more easily than one starting from equilibrium; the same holds
symmetrically for a container already trending toward the entropic
pole). This is a distinct mechanism from §16.1's pooled-aggregation
visibility caveat (loosely, "the straw that breaks the camel's back" as
an observational problem --- a single child's crisis hidden inside a
parent's averaged view until the average itself moves): that concerns
whether a tracker can see a threshold-crossing event as it happens. This
proposition instead concerns the actual causal magnitude of a given
events effect on Ψ\_loyal, which can be amplified by existing debt or
momentum regardless of whether any capacity threshold is being
approached at all, at any point in a cycle sequence, not only at its
terminal event.

Proposition 10.5b.9a.3g (Duration-Independent Impact on an
Already-Vulnerable System). A related but distinct claim from
10.5b.9a.3d's magnitude result: a brief coupling's own effect on an
already-vulnerable container is not governed by that coupling's own
duration, nor by how many prior knocks the container has already
absorbed, but by the container's current vulnerability state at the
moment of contact. A short, transient coupling striking a container
whose internal margin is already narrow (per §10.5b.18.18k's own
threshold-decisiveness logic) can produce an effect fully comparable to
a much longer or more forceful coupling striking the same container,
because the deciding factor is the state the coupling lands on, not the
coupling's own magnitude-over-time. This means neither a brief
coupling's short duration nor a container's own long prior survival
history should be read as evidence of low impact in either direction: a
system that has weathered many knocks is not thereby protected from a
brief new one, if that history has itself left its current margin narrow
rather than resilient.

Definition 10.5b.9a.3e (The Last Collapse). Among a container's repeated
cycles, a real, distinguishable subtype exists: a decisive,
resource-exhausting cycle whose specific outcome (B1 versus B2)
determines whether the container permanently fails or achieves genuine
baseline re-entry, as opposed to the more common chronic reorganizations
that shake and shrink the container without reaching this decisive
branch point. A given containers grid position and hostility may expose
it to more than one such event across its lifetime, each independently
decisive at the time it occurs. A last collapse is identifiable at the
time it occurs, not only in retrospect through failure: a cycle where
hull-breach margin (§10.5a) is near zero and remaining resource reserve
or override capacity is severely depleted is measurably a near-miss last
collapse regardless of whether it resolves toward B2 or B1, since margin
and reserve depletion are directly observable quantities at the time,
independent of the unfalsifiable counterfactual of what would have
happened along some other path. A cycle is correctly classified as
having been a last collapse whether the container survives it via
decisive reset or fails to survive it at all --- survival does not
retroactively disqualify a cycle from having been genuinely decisive;
failure is simply the case where a last collapses outcome happened to
resolve as B1 rather than B2.

10.5b.9a.4 Memory-Reweighting at Reorganization

A B2 reorganization event does more than reset current divergence
(§10.5b.9's B2 outcome, §7.9's stress/debt reset): it changes how the
container's own accumulated history is weighted going forward, at two
distinct timescales.

Definition 10.5b.9a.4a (Reweighting, Not Rewriting). The
history-dependent metric g\_\{C,t\} = g(M\_\{C,t\}) (§4.10) depends on
the container's memory operator, not on the raw historical record
itself. A reorganization event does not alter what actually happened ---
the historical record M\_\{C,t\} is drawing on remains factually
unchanged --- but it can change the weighting function g applies to that
same unchanged history, so that the same past events matter differently
to the current metric after reorganization than they did before.

Definition 10.5b.9a.4b (Micro-Level Mixing Weight, ψ(t)). At the moment
of a B2 event t\_\{B2\}, define ψ(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as g\_new's current
share of influence on the active metric, with the pre-reorganization
weighting g\_old carrying the complementary share:

g\_\{C,t\} = ψ(t)·g\_new(M\_\{C,t\}) + (1−ψ(t))·g\_old(M\_\{C,t\}), t ≥
t\_\{B2\}.

ψ(t) is genuinely discontinuous at t\_\{B2\} itself, jumping from 0 to
some ψ(t\_\{B2\}\^{}+) \textgreater{} 0 rather than approaching
continuously --- the B1/B2 branch point (§10.5b.9) already establishes
this as a real either/or resolution, not a gradual one, and ψ's own
discontinuity is that same resolution's effect on memory-weighting
specifically. Following the jump, ψ(t) undergoes a genuinely
non-monotonic settling window: rather than rising smoothly toward 1 with
noise superimposed on a monotonic trend, ψ(t) can dip back toward ψ ≈ 0
--- the container genuinely reverting to old-self processing for a time
--- before eventually settling, consistent with a real, if temporary,
return to prior patterns during the window immediately following
reorganization.

Proposition 10.5b.9a.4c (Macro-Level Stabilization via Repeated
Cycling). Whether ψ(t)'s settling actually holds, rather than reverting
back toward 0 permanently, is governed by the same cross-cycle process
already established in §10.5b.9a.3: g\_new typically requires multiple
reorganization cycles --- the same n(t) cycle count already governing
whether D\_om itself gets corrected --- to genuinely stabilize, rather
than resolving within a single B2 event. A single successful
reorganization producing a real, if temporary, ψ(t) jump does not by
itself guarantee that g\_new holds permanently; per §10.5b.9a.3's own
two possible long-run resolutions, repeated cycling either eventually
stabilizes g\_new's dominance genuinely, or the container's capacity to
sustain each successive ψ(t) jump is itself eventually exhausted,
consistent with a future cycle resolving as B1 rather than B2.

\hypertarget{sources-of-collapse}{%
\subsection{10.6 Sources of Collapse}\label{sources-of-collapse}}

The collapse operator is generated by multiple coupled mechanisms:
declining informational integrity, accumulated informational stress,
resource depletion, recursive inconsistency, network fragmentation,
maladaptive memory, excessive rigidity. No single variable is
sufficient; collapse is fundamentally multicausal.

\hypertarget{progressive-collapse}{%
\subsection{10.7 Progressive Collapse}\label{progressive-collapse}}

d(d\_A)/dt \textless{} 0, sustained over an interval exceeding a fitted
τ\_prog rather than at any single instant, indicates progressive
collapse. A pointwise negative derivative alone is too easily satisfied
by ordinary, temporary stress in a genuinely healthy system and is not
sufficient on its own --- the same discipline already applied to hull
breach versus confirmed onset (§10.5a) applies here: a single momentary
decline is a fluctuation, not progressive collapse, until it persists.
External failure becomes observable only after adaptive dimensionality
falls below that required for organizational persistence --- this
distinguishes latent degradation from overt failure.

\hypertarget{recovery}{%
\subsection{10.8 Recovery}\label{recovery}}

Define the recovery operator U. Recovery satisfies dim(U(𝒮′))
\textgreater{} dim(𝒮′). Recovery need not restore the original
organization; adaptive systems frequently recover by constructing
entirely new organizational trajectories. §10.5b.9's full-reorganization
outcome (B2) is one concrete route by which U is realized.

\hypertarget{collapse-attractors}{%
\subsection{10.9 Collapse Attractors}\label{collapse-attractors}}

A collapse attractor 𝒜\_C ⊆ 𝒜 is such that X(t) → 𝒜\_C for neighboring
trajectories --- institutional corruption, ecological desertification,
chronic software instability, persistent organizational dysfunction,
long-term physiological dysregulation. Once within a collapse attractor,
ordinary adaptive feedback becomes insufficient for spontaneous
recovery.

\hypertarget{critical-slowing-down}{%
\subsection{10.10 Critical Slowing Down}\label{critical-slowing-down}}

Let τ(X) denote the characteristic recovery time. dτ/d(d\_A) \textless{}
0 --- recovery time increases as adaptive flexibility decreases. This
provides a measurable early-warning indicator of impending collapse.

\hypertarget{recursive-collapse}{%
\subsection{10.11 Recursive Collapse}\label{recursive-collapse}}

Collapse propagates across recursive levels via the recursive coupling
operators of Part 5. Local collapse may remain localized, or propagate
upward, or propagate downward, depending upon recursive coupling
strength.

\hypertarget{collapse-as-loss-of-adaptive-possibility}{%
\subsection{10.12 Collapse as Loss of Adaptive
Possibility}\label{collapse-as-loss-of-adaptive-possibility}}

Collapse is defined as the progressive contraction of the space of
feasible adaptive transformations available to a recursively organized
information-processing system.

\textbf{The Collapse Principle.}

Collapse is the geometric contraction of adaptive possibility. Stability
is the preservation of adaptive possibility. Recovery is the expansion
of adaptive possibility.

\hypertarget{part-11.-derived-observables}{%
\section{Part 11. Derived
Observables}\label{part-11.-derived-observables}}

Observables are functionals defined on the adaptive state manifold, not
primitive variables. Different applications may employ different
empirical measurements while referring to the same underlying
mathematical object.

\hypertarget{observation-functionals}{%
\subsection{11.1 Observation
Functionals}\label{observation-functionals}}

O : 𝒜 → ℝ, or more generally O : 𝒜 → ℝ\^{}m.

\hypertarget{informational-integrity-1}{%
\subsection{11.2 Informational
Integrity}\label{informational-integrity-1}}

I = G(D\_op, D\_om, D\_pm), G monotonically decreasing in each
divergence. High informational integrity indicates coherent internal
organization.

\hypertarget{feedback-permeability-1}{%
\subsection{11.3 Feedback Permeability}\label{feedback-permeability-1}}

Π\_f ∈ {[}0,1{]}. Empirically estimated from learning rates, correction
latency, implementation success, error-resolution frequency, adaptive
responsiveness.

\hypertarget{informational-stress-1}{%
\subsection{11.4 Informational Stress}\label{informational-stress-1}}

S(t) = ∫₀ᵗ K(t, s) (1 − Π\_f(s)) D(s) ds --- inferred from the
interaction between divergence, memory, and permeability. Persistent
high stress predicts increasing adaptive rigidity even before overt
failure becomes visible.

\hypertarget{adaptive-capacity}{%
\subsection{11.5 Adaptive Capacity}\label{adaptive-capacity}}

C\_A := d\_A (φ taken as the identity function). Adaptive capacity is
adaptive degrees of freedom, read as an observable rather than an
internal state variable --- the distinction is one of role, not value.
This measures the size of the feasible adaptive region rather than
instantaneous performance, distinguishing
currently-functioning-but-inflexible systems from genuinely resilient
ones.

\hypertarget{adaptive-rigidity-1}{%
\subsection{11.6 Adaptive Rigidity}\label{adaptive-rigidity-1}}

R\_g = 1 − C̃\_A, where C̃\_A is a normalized adaptive capacity. With
§11.5's C\_A := d\_A, this is C̃\_A = d\_A/d\_A\^{}max, and R\_g = 1 −
d\_A/d\_A\^{}max --- now identical to §10.4's computational default by
construction rather than by coincidence.

\hypertarget{fragmentation}{%
\subsection{11.7 Fragmentation}\label{fragmentation}}

F = Σ\_k {[}w\_k D(P\^{}(k), P\^{}(k+1)){]}, w\_k recursion-level
weights. High fragmentation indicates poor consistency between
neighboring organizational scales --- organizational silos, conflicting
policies, software layer incompatibilities, ecological decoupling.

\textbf{Dependence on network coherence (completing Proposition 12.8's
derivation).}

The per-level discrepancy term D(P\^{}(k), P\^{}(k+1)) is not
independent of how coherent level k's own network is. When N\^{}(k)
(§8.9, §11.8) is low --- level k's containers poorly coordinated among
themselves --- the commensurated inputs σ\_i(P\_i) feeding §5.2's
aggregation are more mutually inconsistent, which directly increases the
discrepancy between the actual parent state and what strict projection
consistency (§5.4) would predict. This gives fragmentation an explicit
dependence on same-level network coherence:

\emph{D}(P\^{}(k), P\^{}(k+1)) = D\_0\^{}(k) + κ\_N · (1 − N\^{}(k)),
κ\_N \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

so that F = clip(Σ\_k w\_k · {[}D\_0\^{}(k) + κ\_N · (1−N\^{}(k)){]}, 0,
1). The clip is required, as Proposition 12.8 shows: with κ\_N
\textgreater{} 0 and N\^{}(k) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, the unclipped sum can exceed
1, which would allow the recursive adaptive-capacity calculation to turn
negative. D\_0\^{}(k) is the level's baseline discrepancy independent of
network coherence (recursive inconsistency of the kind §5.4 already
allows even under perfect coordination, since aggregation is not simple
addition). This is the formula Proposition 12.8 (Part 12) uses to
complete its derivation.

\hypertarget{network-coherence}{%
\subsection{11.8 Network Coherence}\label{network-coherence}}

N = Ψ(G, W, P, R), the same network-resilience functional §8.9 defines
and instantiates concretely. High values correspond to efficient
information propagation, robust resource exchange, resilient
connectivity. Instantiated concretely, and shown identical to a
normalized R\_N, in §8.9.

\hypertarget{resource-sustainability}{%
\subsection{11.9 Resource
Sustainability}\label{resource-sustainability}}

s\_R = liminf\_\{T→∞\} (1/T) ∫₀ᵀ (P\_prod + E\_exch − W − U) dt.
Positive values indicate sustainable adaptation; negative, progressive
resource debt.

\hypertarget{collapse-potential}{%
\subsection{11.10 Collapse Potential}\label{collapse-potential}}

P\_C = Θ(I, S, R, d\_A, N), Θ monotonically increasing in adaptive
degradation. Varies continuously across the adaptive manifold,
estimating systemic vulnerability rather than declaring failure.

\textbf{Computational instantiation.}

\emph{P}\_C(t) = 1 / (1 + exp{[}−(κ₀ + κ₁(1−I(t)) + κ₂S̃(t) +
κ₃(1−Π\_f(t)) + κ₄(1−d\_A(t)/d\_A\^{}max) + κ₅(1−N(t))){]}),

with κ₀ ≤ 0 a fitted baseline, κ₁\ldots κ₅ \textgreater{} 0 fitted
weights, and each bracketed term already defined and normalized to
{[}0,1{]} elsewhere in this document (I: §6.6; S̃: §6.7/§10.3; Π\_f:
§6.5; d\_A: §10.3; N: §8.9). P\_C ∈ (0,1) throughout, increasing in
every source of degradation as required, and has exactly one definition,
used identically whether computed from a Bayesian posterior or a forward
simulation.

\emph{Baseline and interpretation.} Without the baseline, a fully
aligned, fully permeable, unstressed and fully coherent container would
score exactly 0.5. P\_C is a monotonic vulnerability index; it is a
probability of collapse only where it has been calibrated against
observed outcomes in an adapter's own population, with κ₀ set from that
population's base rate.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 11.10.1 (Divergent Signal Flag).}} P\_C(t)
combines several inputs into a single confident score, and that
combination can mask a case where two of its own component signals
genuinely disagree about direction --- most consequentially, severity
(δR̃(t), entering via 1 − d\_A(t)/d\_A\^{}max) reading extreme while
trajectory shape (the trend of d\_A(t) over a trailing window, not
itself one of P\_C's inputs but externally available wherever d\_A(t)'s
full history is) reads mild or improving. Which signal should dominate
in such a case cannot be determined in general --- a real, severe
resource debt with a mild trend can resolve into either a slow-burning
collapse or a genuine, survivable structural burden, and no principled a
priori weighting decides which. Define the flag directly, rather than
resolve it silently within P\_C(t)'s own weighted sum:

Divergent(t) = 𝟙{[}δR̃(t) \textgreater{} θ\_sev{]} · 𝟙{[}trend(d\_A,
trailing window) \textgreater{} θ\_mild{]}, θ\_sev, θ\_mild fitted
(defaults: 85th and 60th percentile of the adapter's own cross-sectional
distribution, respectively).

Where Divergent(t) = 1, P\_C(t) should be reported alongside the flag,
not in place of it, and treated as a lower-confidence estimate
warranting direct review rather than automatic action --- the same
discipline already required of regime indeterminate (§10.5b.1, above): a
confident single score produced where two of its own real inputs
disagree is worse than a flagged, honestly uncertain one, and the engine
should never present the former as though it were the latter.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 11.10.2 (Collapse-Mechanism Signature).}} Not
every signal disagreement is a case of genuine uncertainty about which
should dominate. Some disagreements instead identify \emph{which
collapse mechanism} is active, each with its own characteristic leading
indicator that the others are structurally blind to --- extreme
resource-debt acceleration (§7.9's severity mechanism, operationalized
directly as raw balance-sheet acceleration where available) paired with
\emph{low} contingent-staffing reliance indicates a sudden, debt-driven
collapse in progress: full-time staffing commitments held constant until
a sudden financial failure, with no time for the gradual staffing
response a slower decline would produce. The same extreme severity
paired with \emph{high} contingent-staffing reliance instead indicates
the more common, gradual, enrollment-driven pathway, where declining
revenue is absorbed incrementally through staffing composition well
before any acute crisis. These are not competing readings of the same
underlying risk requiring a weighting decision, the way §11.10.1's
severity-versus-trend case does; they are complementary evidence about
mechanism, and should be reported as such --- a confirmed-high-risk case
should specify which pathway its own signal combination indicates, not
average two mechanism-specific indicators into one undifferentiated
score that fits neither pathway well. Where an adapter cannot supply a
contingent-staffing measure, this distinction cannot be drawn and
Definition 11.10.1 alone governs; where it can, the engine should report
mechanism alongside risk level whenever severity is elevated, rather
than only whether risk is elevated.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 11.10.3 (Delivery-Substitution Signature).}} A
single observable can be produced by two processes this document needs
to distinguish, and neither the observable's level nor its own rate of
change resolves which: growth in the share of instruction delivered
online (a real, adapter-supplied Fall Enrollment quantity, not a
model-derived one) is equally consistent with a genuine, resourced
expansion of access --- more offerings, properly staffed, live
instruction retained --- and with Standing Proxy Divergence's form
substituted for function (§8.5b.5), where cheap, self-paced delivery
quietly replaces genuine instructional engagement while enrollment or
tuition revenue, the visible proxy, keeps looking healthy. Tested alone
against real outcome data, online-share growth showed no discriminating
value at all --- an honest negative result, not a failure of extraction,
since it is measuring a real quantity that is simply silent on which of
the two processes produced it. The distinguishing signal is not the
online-share change itself but whether it moves \emph{together with}
growth in contingent-staffing reliance (§11.10.2's part-time share),
tested as their product rather than either alone:

Substitution(t) = {[}Δ(online\_share){]}₊ · {[}Δ(pt\_share){]}₊, both
changes measured over the same matched interval, with {[}x{]}₊ = max(x,
0): only joint increases score, so a joint decline, whose product would
otherwise be the same positive number as a joint rise, scores zero.

A large, positive value indicates both moving together --- delivery
shifting online while simultaneously shifting toward cheaper,
less-supported staff to deliver it, the hollow, cost-driven version of
expansion. A large online-share increase with near-zero or negative
Δ(pt\_share) indicates the other case --- growth without a corresponding
cheapening of who delivers it, consistent with genuine, resourced
investment rather than substitution. Confirmed directly against real
data: online-share change alone showed zero discriminating value, while
the product term correctly resolved a real institution whose online
growth had been indistinguishable from a distressed pattern using either
signal alone, once its part-time share was shown to have remained flat
rather than risen alongside it. This is structurally the same logic as
Definition 11.10.2 --- two adapter-level observables that are
complementary evidence about which process is occurring, not competing
votes requiring a single weighting decision --- applied to a second,
independently-arising instance of the same underlying problem: a visible
proxy that cannot, on its own, distinguish genuine improvement from its
own hollow imitation.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 11.10.4 (Resource-Specific Trend).}} d\_A(t)'s
own trend (§10.3) is derived from the same official/operational channels
driving the informational side of collapse risk generally; it is not,
and was never intended to be, a resource-debt-specific measure. A real,
substantive gap follows directly from this: a debt-driven collapse can
show extreme, unambiguous δR(t) severity while d\_A(t)'s own trend
remains only mildly negative, because a sudden balance-sheet shock and
gradual informational trajectory are different underlying processes that
need not move in lockstep, and a single enrollment-and-completion-
derived trend has no way to see an acceleration that is happening
specifically in resource-debt terms. Where this gap is suspected ---
severity far exceeding what trend alone would predict, the signature
already named in Definition 11.10.1 --- a resource-specific trend,
computed directly from δR(t)'s own trajectory rather than derived from
d\_A(t), should be checked as a candidate resolving signal before
treating the case as an irreducible exception: δR\_trend(t) = δR(t)'s
own late-window average minus its own mid-window average, mirroring
d\_A\_trend's construction but applied to δR(t) directly rather than
inherited from it. Confirmed directly against real data: a case that had
remained the sole unresolved exception in an otherwise-validated
classifier for the full duration of this document's own real-data
calibration work was resolved by exactly this quantity --- its existing
d\_A\_trend was genuinely flat, while δR\_trend revealed a real,
substantial acceleration invisible to any enrollment-derived measure.
This does not resolve every such case; a second real institution tested
alongside it showed an even larger δR\_trend value while remaining
robustly, stably misclassified under direct bootstrap testing (§12's
stability-testing discipline), confirming a genuine, real signal
conflict rather than small-sample noise. Definition 11.10.4 is
accordingly a real, validated addition to the diagnostic toolkit, not a
universal resolution to every severity-trend mismatch the general
Definition 11.10.1 flags.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 11.10.5 (Scale-Normalized Reserve Adequacy).}}
A container's own resource dynamics (§7.1's Ṙ = P\_prod − W + E\_exch)
and resource debt (§7.9's δR) both measure flow and accumulated deficit;
neither directly captures a container's standing financial cushion ---
the stock of freely available reserves it could draw on before a deficit
becomes existential. Where an adapter can supply this (institutional
endowment or equivalent liquid reserve, for a higher-education
instance), it should be normalized by the container's own structural
scale, not by its resource debt or any other flow quantity:
Reserve\_adequacy(t) = log(1 + Reserve(t) / Scale(C)), where Scale(C) is
a genuine size measure independent of financial flow (population served,
for a higher-education instance). Normalizing by scale rather than debt
is not an arbitrary choice --- confirmed directly against real data,
normalizing by resource debt instead produced a real, substantial
improvement on a smaller, less diverse subset of containers, then broke
once containers spanning several orders of magnitude in raw scale were
included, because a large container's debt and its scale are not
independent: a container three orders of magnitude larger than another
will typically carry proportionally larger absolute debt too, so
dividing reserves by debt partially cancels out exactly the scale
variation the normalization was trying to control for. Dividing by scale
directly, rather than by a flow quantity correlated with scale, resolved
this and correctly separated two real containers whose
severity-and-trend profile alone had placed them in a genuinely
contested region of feature space adjacent to several containers of the
opposite outcome --- confirming this is a real, independent dimension
the other resource-dynamics quantities in this document do not capture,
not a restatement of them under a different name.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 11.10.6 (Generative-Investment Ratio).}} A
container's raw expenditure total says nothing about what that
expenditure is \emph{for} --- two containers spending identically may
differ entirely in whether that spending sustains genuinely generative
activity or merely maintains fixed, non-generative overhead. Where an
adapter can supply a breakdown by real expenditure category, comparing
spending on genuinely generative activity (research output, for a
higher-education instance) against spending on the container's own core
productive function (instruction) is a real, tested, and validated
additional dimension: Generative\_ratio(t) = W\_generative(t) /
W\_core(t). Confirmed directly against real data as a genuine
improvement, correctly resolving a real container that seven other
validated features left misclassified. A structurally adjacent candidate
--- comparing spending on fixed, non-generative overhead (facilities,
auxiliary enterprises) against the same core function --- was tested
alongside it and found to be a genuine, informative negative result
rather than a redundant one: this ratio alone measurably worsened
classification, most plausibly because a large fixed-overhead
expenditure is not consistently directional evidence of either health or
distress on its own --- it can represent a genuinely well-run,
self-sustaining auxiliary operation or a genuine overextension into
fixed cost the container cannot later reduce, and the raw ratio cannot
distinguish the two without a further signal this document does not yet
supply. This is reported alongside the positive result deliberately: a
real, principled hypothesis tested honestly and found not to earn its
place is as much a genuine finding as one that does, and should not be
silently omitted once a companion hypothesis succeeds.

\hypertarget{observable-invariance}{%
\subsection{11.11 Observable Invariance}\label{observable-invariance}}

If two systems satisfy X\_1 \textless{} X\_2 with respect to a
particular adaptive property, any admissible observation procedure
should preserve that ordering up to measurement error.

\emph{\textbf{Admissibility, defined.}} An observation procedure is
admissible for a property X only if its measurement mapping is
order-preserving with respect to X's latent value over the domain under
study, up to stated measurement uncertainty --- an explicit requirement,
not a claim that happens to be true of good procedures by definition.
This principle is accordingly a real constraint on adapter-supplied
measurement mappings (§15.1's representation and measurement mappings
among them), not a tautology: a mapping that fails order-preservation
for X is not admissible for assessing X, regardless of whether it is
otherwise well-constructed for some other purpose.

\emph{\textbf{Establishing admissibility empirically.}}
Order-preservation relative to a latent property cannot always be
checked against the latent property directly, since it is often exactly
what is unobservable. Admissibility is accordingly established through
one or more of: direct validation where the latent quantity has an
independent measurable proxy; calibration experiments comparing the
procedure's output across cases with known relative ordering established
some other way; convergent validity across multiple independent
admissible procedures agreeing on the same ordering; or, where none of
these is available, an explicit, stated structural assumption rather
than an unstated one. This is not a requirement to verify against
unobservable ground truth directly, which would make the criterion
impossible to instantiate; it is a requirement that whichever of these
routes is used be stated, so admissibility claims are checkable rather
than asserted.

\textbf{The Observable Principle.}

Observable quantities are functionals defined on the underlying adaptive
state manifold, revealing informational organization, recursive
structure, resource sustainability, and adaptive possibility in
empirically measurable form.

\hypertarget{part-12.-fundamental-mathematical-results}{%
\section{Part 12. Fundamental Mathematical
Results}\label{part-12.-fundamental-mathematical-results}}

Unless otherwise stated, all statements assume that the informational
dynamics, resource functionals, and recursive operators satisfy the
regularity conditions introduced in the preceding sections, including
the scope conditions on the information manifold specified in §4.1.

\textbf{Theorem 12.1 (Existence of Adaptive Trajectories).}

Let X̄(t) = (P, R, B, M, 𝒢\_net(t)) denote the adaptive state of a
container together with its interaction graph (§10.1), written X(t)
below for brevity. Suppose the governing evolution operators are
continuous and locally Lipschitz on the adaptive state manifold. Then,
for every admissible initial condition X(0) = X\_0, there exists a
unique local adaptive trajectory X(t) defined on some interval {[}0, T).

The hypotheses include continuity in t as well as local Lipschitz
continuity in the state, since the governing equations depend on time
through g\_\{C,t\}, P\_m(t), memory and external forcing. The theorem
covers the deterministic flow; the stochastic version of §6.4 is a
stochastic differential equation whose existence and uniqueness rest on
its own conditions (Lipschitz drift and diffusion with linear growth),
which the default noise law of §6.4 meets on the simplex's interior
given a Lipschitz drift.

Sketch of proof. Between discrete events --- tipping-momentum discharge
(§8.8b), regime-genesis branching (§10.5b.2), and other
threshold-triggered switches this document defines --- the coupled
evolution equations define an ordinary dynamical system on the adaptive
manifold. Under local Lipschitz continuity, the Picard--Lindelöf theorem
guarantees local existence and uniqueness on each such continuous
segment. Recursive coupling does not alter this conclusion provided the
aggregation and projection operators remain continuous within a segment.
Existence and uniqueness across a discrete event itself --- where the
resolution kick's impulsive discharge (§8.8b) or a regime branch
introduces a genuine discontinuity --- requires the applicable
hybrid-system transition condition (a well-defined post-event reset map
at each such threshold) rather than Picard--Lindelöf directly, which
governs only the continuous segments between events. ∎

RICD therefore defines a mathematically well-posed dynamical system on
each continuous segment, with well-posedness across discrete events
following from the applicable transition mechanism's own reset map,
provided that reset map is itself deterministic, single-valued, and
admissible --- this document does not separately prove that property for
every threshold mechanism it defines (regime branching, tipping
discharge, overrides, floods, emergency reinforcement, and others), and
the more such mechanisms a given implementation uses, the less safe it
is to treat global hybrid well-posedness as automatically inherited from
this theorem rather than checked per mechanism. What follows directly
from a named, standard theorem under stated hypotheses is local
well-posedness on continuous segments specifically; global hybrid
well-posedness is a further claim, resting on each individual reset
map's own properties, not established here in general.

\emph{\textbf{Simultaneous threshold events.}} A single reset map's own
determinism does not by itself determine what happens when two or more
distinct threshold conditions become true within the same timestep or
numerical event tolerance --- regime-genesis branching and tipping
discharge triggering together, for instance. These reset operators do
not generally commute (apply-A-then-B need not equal apply-B-then-A), so
an implementation needs an explicit rule, not an assumption that order
is immaterial. The general default: where an adapter's own timestep can
be reduced, simultaneity within tolerance is treated as a numerical
artifact to be resolved by refining the timestep until the events
separate, rather than as a genuine simultaneous occurrence requiring its
own composite rule. Where an adapter's timestep is fixed by the
granularity of its own real data and cannot be meaningfully refined (an
annual reporting cycle, for instance, where no finer-grained observation
exists to resolve which event happened first), a fixed priority ordering
among this document's own threshold mechanisms is the required fallback,
adapter-stated and applied consistently: collapse onset (§10.5a) is
resolved first, since its confirmation criterion is about sustained
state rather than an instantaneous trigger and other mechanisms' reset
maps may depend on whether onset is already confirmed; tipping discharge
and regime-genesis branching are resolved next, in whichever order the
adapter states and holds fixed across its own implementation; overrides,
floods, and emergency reinforcement are resolved last, since each is
itself a response to a divergence-or-stress state that the earlier
mechanisms may have just changed. An implementation that cannot reduce
its timestep and declines to adopt a stated priority ordering has not
fully specified its own trajectory, and two such implementations should
be expected to diverge on data containing genuine near-simultaneous
events --- this is an honest account of what remains adapter
responsibility, not a claim that this document's own hybrid architecture
is complete without it.

Post-reset event closure. The priority ordering above resolves which of
several initially coincident thresholds fires first; it does not by
itself address what happens next, once that reset has actually changed
the state. A reset can change S, δR, d\_A, Π\_f, or another state
variable enough to immediately cross a second threshold, whose own reset
may cross a third, and so on --- a genuine event cascade, and in a
pathological case, one that cycles (reset A pushes the state over B's
threshold, reset B pushes it back over A's) rather than terminating. The
rule: after any discrete reset, all threshold conditions are reevaluated
on the resulting state, and any further event triggered by that state is
resolved according to the same priority ordering already established
above --- this is iteration of the existing rule, not a new one. An
implementation must track the sequence of post-reset states and detect
either a repeated state (a cycle) or a cascade depth exceeding a fitted
maximum (κ\_cascade, adapter-supplied, defaulting to a small integer
such as 5); where neither a stable fixed point nor termination is
reached within that limit, the engine returns hybrid transition
indeterminate for that timestep rather than iterating indefinitely or
silently picking a cycle member as the answer. This is the same
honest-uncertainty discipline already required of regime indeterminate
and the divergent-signal flag: a cascade that cannot be resolved within
a stated bound is reported as unresolved, not forced to a confident but
arbitrary answer.

Cycle detection versus depth exhaustion, distinguished. "Hybrid
transition indeterminate" as currently stated conflates two
diagnostically different situations that should be reported separately
rather than collapsed into one flag. A genuine detected cycle --- the
same state, or a state within tolerance of one already visited,
recurring before κ\_cascade is reached --- confirms the cascade is
structurally caught in a loop, a real fact about the system's own
dynamics. Reaching κ\_cascade's depth limit without ever detecting a
repeated state is a different, weaker finding: the cascade may have been
slowly converging and simply needed more steps than the budget allowed,
or may have been diverging without ever exactly repeating. Reporting
both as the identical "indeterminate" outcome discards this distinction;
the engine should tag which of the two actually occurred ---
cycle\_detected versus depth\_exhausted --- so a downstream user can
tell a structurally confirmed loop from an budget limit that may simply
need raising.

\textbf{Theorem 12.2 (Nonnegativity of Informational Stress).}

Suppose K(t, s) ≥ 0, D(s) ≥ 0, and 0 ≤ Π\_f(s) ≤ 1. Then S(t) = ∫₀ᵗ K(t,
s)(1 − Π\_f(s)) D(s) ds is nonnegative.

\emph{\textbf{Nondecreasing claim, corrected.}} An earlier statement of
this theorem additionally claimed S(t) is nondecreasing wherever
unresolved divergence persists. That claim does not follow from the
hypotheses stated above and is false in general: with K(t,s) =
e\^{}\{−t\} (satisfying K ≥ 0) and (1−Π\_f(s))D(s) = 1 (satisfying both
remaining hypotheses), S(t) = t·e\^{}\{−t\}, which rises to a peak at t
= 1 and strictly decreases thereafter --- nonnegativity holds
throughout, nondecreasing does not. The nonnegativity sketch below only
shows every factor inside a single integral is nonnegative, which does
not by itself compare S at two different values of t when K depends on t
as well as s, since the kernel's own shape, not only the accumulated
area, can change as t increases. The corrected statement: nondecreasing
behavior additionally requires the kernel to be non-forgetting in the
relevant sense, e.g. ∂K/∂t ≥ 0 for fixed s, or equivalently that no
already-accumulated contribution loses weight as t advances. This is the
accumulation regime; it is explicitly not the regime this document's own
S(t) recursion (§10.5b.18, the exponential-decay construction S(t+Δt) =
S(t)·exp(−λΔt) + (1−exp(−λΔt))·(1−Π\_f)·D(t)) uses anywhere else, since
that recursion is a forgetting kernel by design --- older stress is
deliberately down-weighted as time passes, which is the entire point of
using a decay term rather than a running sum. S(t) as actually
constructed and used throughout this document is accordingly
nonnegative, per the sketch below, but not claimed to be nondecreasing;
the two claims should not be conflated.

Sketch of proof (nonnegativity only). Every factor inside the integral
is nonnegative, so the integral is nonnegative; additional unresolved
divergence contributes nonnegative area to the accumulated stress
functional at any single t. ∎

Stress cannot decrease simply because time passes under a non-forgetting
kernel --- it decreases only through genuine reduction of unresolved
informational discrepancy or through an explicit forgetting mechanism
incorporated into the memory kernel. Under a forgetting kernel, such as
the one this document's own S(t) recursion actually uses, stress can and
should decline over time even under some continued unresolved
divergence, as its own contribution ages and is down-weighted --- this
is current memory-weighted stress, a distinct quantity from
non-forgetting accumulated stress, and the two should not be assumed
interchangeable.

\textbf{Proposition 12.3 (Bounded Informational Integrity).}

Suppose G is normalized. Then 0 ≤ I ≤ 1, with I = 1 if and only if all
representation divergences vanish. Perfect informational integrity
corresponds to complete consistency among internal representations; it
does not imply optimal adaptation or maximal performance.

\textbf{Theorem 12.4 (Conditional Resource Feasibility).}

Suppose R(0) ≥ 0, and suppose further that informational work is
self-limiting in the sense that W(t) ≤ w(R(t)) for some function w with
w(0) = 0 and w continuous --- i.e., the container cannot perform work it
lacks the resources to fund, rather than merely being assumed not to. If
P\_prod + E\_exch ≥ 0 whenever R is at or near zero, then R(t) ≥ 0 for
all admissible times. §7.3's concrete instantiation --- maintenance
funded first, adaptive work clipped to what remains, W(t) =
W\_adaptive(t) + M\_maint\^{}actual(t) ≤ R(t) --- satisfies w(R) = R
exactly for total work, so any implementation using that clipping rule
automatically satisfies this theorem's work-bound hypothesis; the
theorem's remaining, separate hypothesis on P\_prod + E\_exch near R = 0
concerns externally imposed extraction the clipping rule does not
govern, and must still be verified for the specific application.

\emph{Units and scope.} Under the per-timestep convention of §7.3, the
work bound reads W(t)·Δt ≤ w(R(t)). The theorem applies to the core
balance of §7.4; with §7.7's recovery term the boundary condition
becomes P\_prod + E\_exch + ρ·R\_target ≥ 0 at R = 0.

Sketch of proof. Consider the boundary R = 0. There, w(0) = 0, so W ≤ 0
forces W = 0 by nonnegativity of work (Axiom 3 gives strict positivity
only for nontrivial updates; at the boundary no work can be funded).
Hence dR/dt = P\_prod − 0 + E\_exch ≥ 0 by hypothesis, so R cannot be
driven below zero from R = 0: the boundary is repelling or at worst
reflecting. By continuity, R(t) ≥ 0 follows on any interval where the
hypotheses hold. ∎

This is a genuine, if narrower, feasibility result: nonnegativity is
derived from a stated mechanism (work is bounded by available resource)
rather than assumed as the hypothesis that also serves as the
conclusion. An application that cannot justify W ≤ w(R) with w(0) = 0
has not established resource feasibility and should not cite this
theorem.

\textbf{Proposition 12.5 (Adaptive-Dimensional Contraction Under the
Collapse Definition).}

If 𝒬 : 𝒮 → 𝒮′ is the collapse operator, then dim(𝒮′) \textless{} dim(𝒮),
by Definition 10.1 directly. This is definitional rather than a further
derived result, and is recorded here as a proposition rather than a
theorem for that reason.

\emph{\textbf{Bridging the scalar and the geometric.}} d\_A(t) (§10.3)
is a scalar quantity; 𝒬's dimensional contraction above is a geometric
one, and nothing so far states what relationship licenses using a
decline in the former as evidence of the latter. The bridge: the theory
requires only that d\_A(t) be an order-preserving scalar measure of
effective adaptive possibility --- not that it simultaneously be volume,
rank, and measure at once, which are not generally equivalent under
coordinate transformation or aggregation. Weighted volume, rank, and
related measures are alternative, domain-specific instantiations an
adapter may choose among, not three simultaneously-identical
interpretations of a single mathematical object; a given implementation
commits to one. What every instantiation shares is measuring the
feasible adaptive transformations genuinely available within a
container's state space, not the topological dimension of the ambient
state manifold itself. A decline in d\_A(t) accordingly represents
contraction of the effectively \emph{accessible} portion of that
manifold, even where the manifold's own formal ambient dimension is
unchanged; a two-dimensional region can shrink in area continuously
without becoming one-dimensional, and d\_A(t) tracks the shrinking area,
not a change in topological dimension. §10.7's dd\_A/dt \textless{} 0
criterion and 𝒬's definitional dim(𝒮′) \textless{} dim(𝒮) are
accordingly related but distinct claims operating at different levels of
the same contraction: one is the observable, scalar symptom; the other
is what has actually occurred geometrically once contraction is severe
and sustained enough to constitute confirmed collapse rather than
ordinary fluctuation.

\textbf{Recovery Criterion 12.6 (Recovery Expands Adaptive
Possibility).}

If recovery succeeds, dim(U(𝒮′)) \textgreater{} dim(𝒮′), again following
from the definition of U in §10.8. Recovery is mathematically distinct
from returning to a previous state; adaptive systems frequently recover
through novel organizational structures rather than exact restoration.

\textbf{Proposition 12.7 (Recursive Consistency).}

If every aggregation operator preserves informational compatibility,
every included recursion level carries a positive weight (w\_k
\textgreater{} 0), and the divergence functional D used to construct F
is genuinely identity-separating (D(P,Q) = 0 if and only if P = Q,
available for some but not all of Definition 4.2's admissible
functionals given the definitions deliberately broad domain) --- three
hypotheses stated explicitly here rather than left implicit in
``preserves informational compatibility'' alone, since that condition by
itself is not sufficient to establish the only-if direction --- then
recursive inconsistency F = 0 if and only if every recursion level
possesses identical informational organization after projection. Perfect
recursive consistency is a limiting case; real adaptive systems
generally possess nonzero but bounded recursive inconsistency.

\textbf{Proposition 12.8 (Network Fragmentation and Adaptive Capacity).}

§10.3's instantiation gives d\_A(t) = d\_A\^{}max·exp{[}−β\_S S̃ − β\_R
δR̃ − β\_F F̃{]}, so:

∂d\_A/∂F̃ = −β\_F · d\_A \textless{} 0 for β\_F \textgreater{} 0, d\_A
\textgreater{} 0.

§11.7 gives F̃ (normalized F) an explicit dependence on network
coherence: F = clip(Σ\_k w\_k·{[}D\_0\^{}(k) + κ\_N·(1−N\^{}(k)){]}, 0,
1), the clip added here because κ\_N \textgreater{} 0 and N\^{}(k) ∈
{[}0,1{]} alone do not bound each component to {[}0,1{]} --- D\_0\^{}(k)
= 0.4 with κ\_N = 1 at N\^{}(k) = 0 already gives 1.4 for that level
before weighting, and the weighted sum could exceed 1 without an
explicit bound, which would drive 1−F negative and make d\_A(C*) itself
negative, contaminating every downstream quantity that treats d\_A as a
nonnegative adaptive-possibility measure. The clip is a deliberate,
stated choice rather than a silent patch: an adapter that instead wants
D\_0\^{}(k) + κ\_N·(1−N\^{}(k)) ≤ 1 to hold by construction may impose
that as a parameter constraint on κ\_N directly, rather than relying on
the clip, but the clip is required as the default so that F̃ ∈ {[}0,1{]}
is guaranteed regardless of which route an adapter takes. ∂F/∂N\^{}(k) =
−κ\_N·w\_k \textless{} 0 for every level k with w\_k \textgreater{} 0,
away from the clip boundary. Both links are now explicit: worsening
fragmentation (dF̃/dt \textgreater{} 0) follows from declining network
coherence at any weighted level (dN\^{}(k)/dt \textless{} 0) via §11.7's
formula, and, at the level of partial dependence, ∂d\_A/∂F̃ \textless{} 0
establishes that fragmentation's contribution to d\_A's total derivative
is monotonically negative. Consequently, holding the remaining
fragmentation components (the D\_0\^{}(k) terms and weights w\_k) fixed,
or absent compensating changes elsewhere in F sufficient to offset the
coherence term's own contribution, dN\^{}(k)/dt \textless{} 0 at any
weighted level implies dF̃/dt \textgreater{} 0, as a genuine derivation
from two stated equations rather than one stated equation and one
narrative claim. Without that qualification the implication does not
follow cleanly, since dF̃/dt is a sum of terms and is not determined by
the coherence term alone if other components move to counteract it.

Deferred decision, noted explicitly. Whether the hard clip above is the
right functional form --- versus a smooth saturation approaching 1
rather than an abrupt bound at it --- is deliberately left undecided
here rather than resolved by preference. No real data currently exists
to decide it: the live higher-education tracker holds F\_t at zero
throughout by stated, honest design (§15's own documented simplification
for its current single-container reduced model), so no institution's
raw, pre-clip fragmentation value has ever actually been computed
against real data. This decision is deferred specifically until a future
tracker implements genuine multi-container network fragmentation and can
be tested against real cases --- at which point the actual distribution
of raw F values near the {[}0,1{]} boundary, and whether anything
downstream depends on dF/dt's continuity there, can settle the question
on real grounds rather than a priori preference for simplicity or
smoothness.

\emph{\textbf{The total derivative requires the same qualification a
second time, at the next link.}} d\_A(t) depends on three arguments ---
S̃, δR̃, and F̃ --- and its total time derivative is d(d\_A)/dt =
(∂d\_A/∂S̃)·Ṡ̃ + (∂d\_A/∂δR̃)·δṘ̃ + (∂d\_A/∂F̃)·F̃̇. Declining coherence, via
dF̃/dt \textgreater{} 0 above, guarantees a negative contribution through
the third term alone; it does not by itself guarantee d(d\_A)/dt
\textless{} 0, since sufficiently improving stress or resource debt in
the same interval could offset it. The correct statement: declining
network coherence contributes monotonically negatively to d(d\_A)/dt
through fragmentation's own term, holding S̃ and δR̃ fixed or absent
compensating changes in them sufficient to offset that contribution; the
net sign of d(d\_A)/dt in general depends on simultaneous changes in all
three arguments, not on fragmentation alone. This is the same ``all else
equal'' qualification already required one link earlier in this chain,
applied a second time at the point where the chain actually terminates
in d\_A itself.

This restoration, like Theorem 12.4's, is conditional on accepting
§10.3's exponential form for d\_A and §11.7's linear-in-incoherence form
for D(P\^{}(k),P\^{}(k+1)) as the operative definitions for a given
application. Applications using different (independently justified)
functional forms for either would need to re-derive this coupling for
those forms; the proposition's status is tied to the default
instantiations, not proven for every conceivable pair of choices.

\textbf{Corollary 12.9 (Positive Feedback Instability).}

If informational divergence increases informational work, resource
depletion reduces adaptive updating, and reduced adaptive updating
increases divergence, then the coupled system admits positive-feedback
regimes in which dD/dt \textgreater{} 0 and dR/dt \textless{} 0
simultaneously reinforce one another. This does not imply inevitable
collapse; external intervention, resource infusion, or successful
informational correction may interrupt the cycle.

\textbf{Theorem 12.10 (Scale Invariance of the Formalism).}

If the recursive operators satisfy the axioms of Part 5, the governing
equations retain their form under changes of recursion level; only the
interpretation of the variables changes. The same mathematical framework
therefore applies to cells, organisms, organizations, institutions,
ecosystems, and software systems without modification of the governing
formalism. Scale invariance here means invariance of the governing
formal architecture specifically --- not invariance of parameter values,
state-space representations, commensuration maps, or observational
resolution across scales, all of which remain adapter-supplied and
genuinely different from one recursion level to the next. The equations
keep their form; what those equations are evaluated against does not
automatically transfer between levels. Given this explicit narrowing,
the name is retained deliberately rather than switched to a term like
``recursive form invariance'' or ``scale-general architecture'': this
document's specific, stated meaning is invariance of the governing
formal architecture under recursion, and calling that scale invariance
is accurate under the definition given here, not a redefinition intended
to borrow the rhetorical weight of the mathematically stronger,
unqualified sense of the term. A reader who takes ``scale invariance''
to promise parameter, representation, or resolution invariance as well
is reading past the qualification stated directly above, not finding an
error the qualification failed to address.

\textbf{The General Persistence Criterion}

\emph{\textbf{Criterion 12.G.}} (renumbered from Criterion 12.1 --- the
original number collided with Theorem 12.1's existence-and-uniqueness
result earlier in this Part, inviting exactly the kind of ``12.1 meaning
which one'' ambiguity this document has otherwise worked to eliminate at
the symbol level.)

A recursively organized adaptive system remains viable provided:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  informational integrity remains bounded away from zero;
\item
  adaptive resources remain nonnegative and sustainable;
\item
  adaptive degrees of freedom do not contract below the minimum required
  for continued organization;
\item
  recursive coupling preserves sufficient coherence across scales;
\item
  network connectivity remains capable of supporting informational and
  resource exchange.
\end{itemize}

Failure of one criterion need not imply collapse. Persistent degradation
across several criteria, however, contracts adaptive possibility and
drives the system toward collapse.

\textbf{The Fundamental Result of RICD.}

Adaptive persistence emerges from the coupled evolution of informational
organization, resource sustainability, recursive structure, network
connectivity, and historical memory. Collapse corresponds to the
progressive contraction of adaptive possibility produced by failures
across these interacting domains.

\hypertarget{part-13.-relationship-to-existing-mathematical-frameworks}{%
\section{Part 13. Relationship to Existing Mathematical
Frameworks}\label{part-13.-relationship-to-existing-mathematical-frameworks}}

\hypertarget{relation-to-classical-dynamical-systems}{%
\subsection{13.1 Relation to Classical Dynamical
Systems}\label{relation-to-classical-dynamical-systems}}

RICD enlarges the notion of state to X̄ = (P, R, B, M, 𝒢\_net),
incorporating informational organization, resource dynamics, boundaries,
historical memory, and adaptive networks. Classical dynamical systems
generally assume fixed state variables; RICD allows both the variables
and the organizational architecture through which they interact to
evolve recursively.

\hypertarget{relation-to-information-geometry}{%
\subsection{13.2 Relation to Information
Geometry}\label{relation-to-information-geometry}}

RICD adopts the geometric perspective of information geometry as its
informational foundation, subject to the scope conditions of §4.1, and
departs from it by coupling the informational manifold to recursive
organization, adaptive resources, and evolving interaction networks, and
by making the geometry itself history-dependent (Definition 4.8, §4.10;
consequences for identity developed in §9.10 and completed in §10.5b.0).
Traditional information geometry studies geometry on probability spaces;
RICD studies adaptive systems whose geometry itself evolves.

\hypertarget{relation-to-network-science}{%
\subsection{13.3 Relation to Network
Science}\label{relation-to-network-science}}

RICD treats network topology as an emergent adaptive quantity rather
than a fixed modeling assumption. Conventional network models typically
evolve node states on a graph; RICD simultaneously evolves both the node
states and the graph itself.

\hypertarget{relation-to-control-theory}{%
\subsection{13.4 Relation to Control
Theory}\label{relation-to-control-theory}}

FDFM extends feedback beyond an external controller to an intrinsic
property of every adaptive container, with feedback effectiveness
depending upon informational integrity, permeability, memory, and
available resources rather than controller design alone.

\hypertarget{relation-to-cybernetics}{%
\subsection{13.5 Relation to
Cybernetics}\label{relation-to-cybernetics}}

RICD may be viewed as a mathematical continuation of the cybernetic
tradition, developing communication, adaptation, recursive organization,
and self-regulation within a formal framework based on information
geometry, recursive operators, adaptive resources, and evolving
networks. One classical cybernetic result deserves explicit connection:
Ashby's Law of Requisite Variety, that a regulator can only control a
system if the regulator's own variety is at least as great as the
disturbance it must counter. Π\_f(t) (§6.4, §10.5b) is this document's
quantitative development of that same concern, applied specifically to
whether corrective information can penetrate a container's own structure
rather than merely exist alongside it --- §13.7 develops this connection
further.

\hypertarget{relation-to-statistical-mechanics}{%
\subsection{13.6 Relation to Statistical
Mechanics}\label{relation-to-statistical-mechanics}}

The recursive aggregation operators of Part 5 permit genuine emergence
rather than simple averaging --- higher-order containers acquire new
informational states, resource dynamics, and boundary operators not
generally reducible to microscopic descriptions alone.

\hypertarget{relation-to-complexity-science}{%
\subsection{13.7 Relation to Complexity
Science}\label{relation-to-complexity-science}}

RICD's principal contribution is a common mathematical language for
recursive hierarchy, adaptive networks, informational organization,
resource economics, historical memory, collapse, and recovery, derived
from a common set of primitive objects and axioms rather than
independent models per phenomenon.

\emph{\textbf{A note on what this contribution is not.}} The claim below
is a synthesis claim, not a discovery claim. Every distinction this
subsection identifies as central to RICD already exists somewhere in the
complexity-science, network-science, cybernetic, or organizational
literature, individually and separately named. RICD's contribution is
not having noticed a distinction the field has missed; it is treating a
specific set of these already-known distinctions as jointly necessary,
formalizing them within one coupled dynamical system, and supplying a
specific mechanism --- the official/operational/meta-representation
split of §6.1 --- for how they reinforce one another rather than merely
co-occurring.

\textbf{Distinctions RICD keeps formally separate, and their existing
antecedents.}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \emph{Connectivity is not integration.} A network can remain
  physically connected while the responses its parts produce cease to be
  mutually compatible. This echoes the structural-versus-functional
  connectivity distinction well established in network neuroscience,
  where the physical wiring of a network and the statistical or causal
  dependencies actually observed in its activity are tracked as separate
  quantities. RICD's analogue is Λ\_ij(t) (coupling strength, §8.5)
  versus D\_op(t)/D\_om(t)/D\_pm(t) (§6.3): a coupling can persist at
  full strength while what flows across it diverges.
\item
  \emph{Feedback existing is not feedback being corrective.} A system
  can be saturated with communication --- complaints, sensors, reports
  --- without that information being able to alter the system's actual
