minus entropy; the cross-entropy terms sum to H(M)). On a two-point
space the mixture of Bern(a) and Bern(b) is Bern(m), which gives the
formula. Boundedness and the equality cases hold for base-2
Jensen--Shannon divergence generally. H\_2 is differentiable on (0, 1)
with H\_2′(p) = log₂((1 − p)/p), strictly decreasing and unbounded at
both ends; differentiating the formula gives the partial derivative,
which is positive when a \textgreater{} m (that is, a \textgreater{} b),
negative when a \textless{} b, and 0 when a = b. As a → 0 with b fixed
in (0, 1), H\_2′(a) → +∞ while H\_2′(m) stays bounded, so the derivative
diverges; on the diagonal m = a and the two terms cancel. Continuity at
the endpoints extends strict monotonicity to the closed intervals. ∎

Smoothing and regularity. The values used as tracker features are
computed unsmoothed: two-point divergences between rates are finite for
all inputs, because the mixture has positive mass wherever either
argument does. Their derivatives, however, are unbounded near the
boundary (Lemma 15.2.1), and the price, four-year and soundness gaps
reach the boundary (a college whose students pay nothing, whose
completers all finish late, or whose revenue covers none of its
expenses). Wherever these divergences enter the gradient flow of §6.4,
either Definition 6.2's smoothing is applied with a fixed ε
\textgreater{} 0, keeping every argument in the interior, or Definition
6.1's derivative-free update is declared (Proposition 6.3.4e). The
tracker's own use of them, as computed features with no gradient taken
through them, needs neither.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.2 (The Discount Function).}}

For x ∈ {[}0, 1{]}, the divergence between a claim that the full amount
is realized and a realization of the fraction 1 − x is

g\_JS(x) = JS( Bern(1), Bern(1 − x) ) = H\_2(1 − x/2) − ½·H\_2(1 − x).

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 15.2.3 (Properties of the Discount
Function).}}

g\_JS(0) = 0 and g\_JS(1) = 1; g\_JS is strictly increasing and strictly
convex on {[}0, 1{]}, with

g\_JS′(x) = ½·log₂( (2 − x)/(1 − x) ) for 0 ≤ x \textless{} 1, g\_JS″(x)
= 1 / ( 2·ln 2 · (1 − x)(2 − x) ) \textgreater{} 0,

so g\_JS′(0) = ½, g\_JS(x) = x/2 + O(x²) near 0, the slope grows without
bound as x → 1, and g\_JS(2x) \textgreater{} 2·g\_JS(x) for 0
\textless{} x ≤ ½. For example, g\_JS(0.1) ≈ 0.052, g\_JS(0.3) ≈ 0.169
and g\_JS(0.5) ≈ 0.311. The form

g\_JS(x) = ½·{[} −log₂(1 − x/2) + (1 − x)·log₂( (1 − x)/(1 − x/2) ) + x
{]}

is an equivalent closed form of the same function. {[}Derived{]}

Proof. H\_2(1) = 0 and H\_2(½) = 1 give the endpoint values.
Differentiating, g\_JS′(x) = −½·H\_2′(1 − x/2) + ½·H\_2′(1 − x) =
−½·log₂( (x/2)/(1 − x/2) ) + ½·log₂( x/(1 − x) ) = ½·log₂( (2 − x)/(1 −
x) ), positive on {[}0, 1). Differentiating again, g\_JS″(x) = (1/(2 ln
2))·{[} 1/(1 − x) − 1/(2 − x) {]} = 1/(2 ln 2 · (1 − x)(2 − x)),
positive on {[}0, 1). Strict monotonicity on the closed interval follows
by continuity at x = 1. For the doubling inequality, strict convexity
and g\_JS(0) = 0 give g\_JS(x) = g\_JS( ½·0 + ½·2x ) \textless{}
½·g\_JS(2x). For the equivalence of the two forms, expand the entropy
form: H\_2(1 − x/2) = −(1 − x/2)·log₂(1 − x/2) − (x/2)·log₂ x + x/2, and
½·H\_2(1 − x) = −½(1 − x)·log₂(1 − x) − ½·x·log₂ x, so

g\_JS(x) = −(1 − x/2)·log₂(1 − x/2) + ½(1 − x)·log₂(1 − x) + x/2.

The second form expands to −½·log₂(1 − x/2) + ½(1 − x)·log₂(1 − x) − ½(1
− x)·log₂(1 − x/2) + x/2, and −½ − ½(1 − x) = −(1 − x/2), so the two
agree term by term. ∎

Remark (reading the scale). Small shortfalls register at about half
their size, and a shortfall that doubles more than doubles the
divergence (g\_JS(2x) \textgreater{} 2·g\_JS(x)). Thresholds on g\_JS
are therefore not thresholds on the raw shortfall, and any threshold is
stated on the scale on which it was fixed.

\textbf{The price gap}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.4 (Inputs).}}

For a college at fiscal year t: S\_post(t) \textgreater{} 0, the
published price, tuition and required fees weighted by the share of
students paying in-state and out-of-state rates (IPEDS Institutional
Characteristics); N\_net(t) ≥ 0, net tuition revenue per
full-time-equivalent student (IPEDS Finance; the field for each
reporting form is verified against each year's data dictionary before
building); the raw realization ratio r\_raw(t) = N\_net(t)/S\_post(t),
reported unclipped for every college; and the clipped discount rate

δ\_disc(t) = 1 − r\_raw(t), clipped to {[}0, 1{]}.

N\_net is an institutional aggregate: differences in enrollment mix, the
full-time-equivalent calculation, fee treatment and reporting practice
all affect it, so r\_raw is the institutional net-tuition realization
ratio, used as a proxy for the fraction of the published price realized,
not the price any individual student pays. Validity rule, fixed with the
v4 protocol before any test college is scored: r\_raw within {[}r\_lo,
r\_hi{]} (defaults 0.05 and 1.10) is valid; a valid r\_raw above 1,
clipped to a zero discount, is retained and flagged; r\_raw outside
{[}r\_lo, r\_hi{]} fails the rule and makes the price dimension
unavailable for that year, with the reason recorded. The flag stays
attached to the price-gap value in every output and analysis that uses
it, not only in a separate data-quality report, so that an unusual ratio
cannot pass as an ordinary zero gap.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.5 (Need-Associated Discount).}}

δ\_need(t) ∈ (0, 1) is the discount associated with the college's
student need profile in the development sample. Default specification,
fixed with the v4 protocol before any test college is scored: target
δ\_disc; predictors the shares of the college's first-time, full-time
federal-aid recipients in each IPEDS family-income band (or, where the
bands are missing, a separate fit on the Pell share), with the college's
control (public, private nonprofit, for-profit) as an additional
predictor; a fractional logit fit, so predictions lie in (0, 1); one
observation per college-year, unweighted; fitted on the earlier years of
the development colleges and validated on the later years (the time
split of the channel-selection stages). The Pell-share model is a
separate fit with its own diagnostics, not a substitution into the
income-band model; each fit is estimated only on college-years meeting
its own missing-data requirements, and every output records which fit
produced δ\_need. Where neither the bands nor the Pell share is
available, ι\_price(t) = 0. No direction is assumed for how need relates
to any outcome; student background enters only here, as a calibrator.
Because the fit learns from observed discounting, δ\_need measures the
discount associated with a need profile under the development sample's
practice, including whatever practice is common across colleges of the
same control, not the discount that need objectively requires; the price
gap therefore measures discounting beyond what is usual for colleges
serving similar students. If the development sample's practice is itself
poor for some need profile, the fit absorbs it: the price gap can
identify discounting that departs from comparable practice, but cannot
establish independently that a college charges more than its students'
need warrants, and P1 and every later reading of the feature keep that
limitation. {[}Model-derived{]}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.6 (Excess Discount and the Price Gap).}}

The need-adjusted price is S\_post·(1 − δ\_need). The realization ratio
against it is

r\_paid(t) = (1 − δ\_disc(t)) / (1 − δ\_need(t)),

the excess discount is

δ\_ex(t) = max\{ 0, 1 − r\_paid(t) \} = max\{ 0, (δ\_disc(t) −
δ\_need(t)) / (1 − δ\_need(t)) \},

and the price gap is

D\_op\^{}price(t) = g\_JS( δ\_ex(t) ) = JS( Bern(1), Bern( min\{1,
r\_paid(t)\} ) ).

ι\_price(t) = 1 where S\_post, N\_net and a need profile are supplied,
r\_raw(t) passes the validity rule, and 1 − δ\_need(t) ≥ η\_pr, a fixed
floor (default 0.05). {[}Model-derived: the ratio form keeps the claim
and the realization in the same units, as Definition 6.1 requires, and
gives the price gap the same form as the four-year and soundness gaps;
it coincides with the difference form δ\_disc − δ\_need when δ\_need =
0{]}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 15.2.7 (Properties of the Price Gap).}}

(a) D\_op\^{}price ∈ {[}0, 1{]}. (b) D\_op\^{}price = 0 if and only if
δ\_disc ≤ δ\_need. (c) D\_op\^{}price = 1 if and only if δ\_disc = 1.
(d) For fixed δ\_need, D\_op\^{}price is nondecreasing in δ\_disc, and
strictly increasing on {[}δ\_need, 1{]}. (e) Scaling the published price
and net tuition by the same factor leaves the price gap unchanged.
{[}Derived{]}

Proof. δ\_ex ∈ {[}0, 1{]} because r\_paid ≥ 0, so (a) follows from
Proposition 15.2.3. (b) and (c): g\_JS vanishes only at 0 and equals 1
only at 1, δ\_ex = 0 exactly when r\_paid ≥ 1 (that is, δ\_disc ≤
δ\_need), and δ\_ex = 1 exactly when r\_paid = 0 (that is, δ\_disc = 1).
(d) δ\_ex is nondecreasing in δ\_disc and equals (δ\_disc − δ\_need)/(1
− δ\_need) on {[}δ\_need, 1{]}, and g\_JS is strictly increasing. (e)
δ\_disc depends on N\_net and S\_post only through their ratio. ∎

Property (e) is the reason for the redesign: the v1 to v3 official
channel was the published price itself, so a college's price level
entered the divergence directly, and the reanalysis weights showed that
divergence built that way carried little closure signal of its own.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.8 (Excess-Discount Widening, Recorded
Separately).}}

With t₀ the first fiscal year of the college's scoring window at which
ι\_price = 1, the widening is Δ\_ex(t) = δ\_ex(t) − δ\_ex(t₀). Δ\_ex is
computed only over years in which the price inputs are defined the same
way (the same finance field and the same published-price weighting) and
the same frozen δ\_need fit applies; availability of other dimensions
does not restrict it. It is recorded for every college and tested
against prediction P1, and is not a feature of the v4 model; if P1 holds
in v4, Δ\_ex may become a feature in v5 under a protocol fixed in
advance. {[}Model-derived{]}

\textbf{The outcome gap}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.9 (Reported Completion Band).}}

C\_rep(t) ∈ {[}0, 1{]} is the completion rate the college reports for an
entering cohort (IPEDS Outcome Measures award rate where available,
otherwise the Graduation Rates completion within 150\% of normal time).
The two sources need not cover the same cohort or count awards the same
way, so the source of C\_rep is recorded, carried into every analysis as
a stratification variable, and a change of source starts a new
comparability segment. τ\_out must come from the same source, entering
cohort, denominator and outcome window as C\_rep, and counts students
who left without an award, disjoint from those counted in C\_rep (the
graduation-rate files count a transfer-out only if the student did not
complete); where either condition fails, τ\_out is treated as
unreported.

The sign of a transfer depends on the claim. The graduation rate counts
students who transfer out as non-completers. At a college whose official
mission includes preparing students to transfer, a student who transfers
and succeeds is the claim kept, not a student lost; at a college whose
claim is that its students complete there, a transfer-out is at best
ambiguous. A transfer-mission college, ι\_tm = 1, is classified by a
rule fixed in the v4 protocol before any college is scored and
independent of what it reports: its Carnegie Basic Classification, in
the edition fixed in the protocol, is an associate's-college category
with a transfer orientation, or its mission statement (collected by
IPEDS), coded under rules fixed in advance, names preparation for
transfer as part of its purpose. Reporting a transfer-out rate is a
separate availability requirement, not evidence of the mission: IPEDS
requires transfer-out data from institutions whose mission includes
substantial preparation for transfer, but allows others to report it
voluntarily, so reporting is necessary for mission colleges and not
sufficient to identify them. For a transfer-mission college with
post-transfer outcomes available (Definition 15.2.11a), let π\_succ be
the share of its covered transfers who succeeded at the receiving
institution and c\_trn ∈ {[}0, 1{]} the share of its transfer-outs those
outcomes cover; otherwise set c\_trn = 0. The official claim is the set
claim

𝒞\_o\^{}outcome = \{ Bern(c) : c ∈ {[}C\_low, C\_high{]} \}, C\_low =
C\_rep + τ\_out·c\_trn·π\_succ, C\_high = min\{ 1, C\_rep + τ\_out·(
c\_trn·π\_succ + 1 − c\_trn ) \}

where τ\_out is reported, and C\_low = C\_rep, C\_high = 1 where it is
not.

Covered transfers count by their outcome, successes as completions and
failures as non-completions; uncovered transfers, whose outcomes are
unknown, remain a band, counted as non-completions at C\_low and as
successes at C\_high. With c\_trn = 0 (a college without a transfer
mission, or with no post-transfer outcomes) this is the
transfer-agnostic band {[}C\_rep, min\{1, C\_rep + τ\_out\}{]}; with
c\_trn = 1 the band collapses to the point C\_rep + τ\_out·π\_succ.
Where τ\_out is unreported, nothing bounds the transfers, so the claim
is consistent with any completion from C\_rep up. The band is non-empty,
closed and convex in every case, so Definition 6.3.4's set-claim
mathematics, and Proposition 15.2.12, apply unchanged.
{[}Model-derived{]}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.10 (External Outcomes and Externally
Calibrated Expected Completion).}}

For e ∈ E\_ext: Q\_lic, the licensure pass rate over the college's
licensed programs, weighted by test-takers (teacher certification under
federal Title II, the CPA examination by school, bar passage, and
nursing licensure through state boards, each verified for coverage and
history before building); and Q\_ret, the retention rate of the same
entering cohort (IPEDS), flagged in every output that uses it because
the college itself reports it. For each e, the externally calibrated
expected completion is

Q*\_e = h\_e( Q\_e ),

where h\_e : {[}0, 1{]} → {[}0, 1{]} is a nondecreasing calibration: the
completion rate the development colleges with that external outcome
report, on average. Q*\_e is therefore not an independently established
completion rate; it is what reported completion would be if the college
followed the development sample's relationship between the external
outcome and reported completion, and the outcome gap measures departures
from that relationship. Wherever the licensure measure attains the
outcome group's value, the share of the college's completers in the
licensed programs it covers is reported with it, since a pass rate over
a small, atypical set of programs is not broad institutional evidence.
Source status: licensure comes from outside examining or licensing
bodies; retention is reported by the college. The two do not carry equal
evidentiary weight, so the source status of the measure attaining the
outcome group's value is shown wherever that value appears, including in
model analyses, and a result driven by retention is reported as such.
Requirements, fixed with the v4 protocol: h\_e is fitted against C\_low
as defined for each college (which is C\_rep wherever c\_trn = 0), so
that the calibrated completion and the band share a base; h\_e is fitted
on the earlier years of the development colleges only, evaluated out of
sample on the later years with its own diagnostics for each e, and then
frozen, so that it cannot learn the reporting patterns of the colleges
it is used to test; retention is taken for the same entering cohort as
C\_rep, and licensure over the exam years in which that cohort's
completers sit the exams, by a rule fixed in advance; licensure is
available for a college only where its licensed-program test-takers in
those years number at least n\_min (default 30), and a college with only
some licensed programs has Q\_lic computed over those programs, with the
share of its completers they cover reported (from completers of the same
cohort where the data allow; otherwise from the same years' completions
by program, labeled a coverage proxy). Pass rates on different
examinations are not comparable as they stand (difficulty, repeat-taker
rules and candidate selection differ), so licensure is calibrated within
exam category: h\_lic,k is fitted separately for each category k
(teacher certification, accountancy, bar, nursing, and any further
category fixed in advance), Q*\_lic,k = h\_lic,k(Q\_lic,k), and Q*\_lic
is the test-taker-weighted mean of the calibrated Q*\_lic,k, the weights
being counts, not fitted values. For each licensure source the protocol
states, before building: whether its rate is for first-time takers or
all attempts (first-time takers are used wherever the source reports
them); how candidates are attributed to the college; how repeat attempts
are treated; how the exam window is assigned to the entering cohort; and
what happens when the source's reporting convention changes (a change
starts a new comparability segment). A source whose candidates cannot be
aligned with the cohort being assessed is used, if at all, as a proxy
with that limitation stated in every output, not as a cohort-matched
rate. ι\_(outcome, e)(t) = 1 where Q\_e and C\_rep are supplied and
these requirements are met. {[}Model-derived{]}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.11 (The Outcome Gap, Overclaiming and
Underclaiming).}}

For each available e, the outcome gap on dimension (outcome, e) is the
distance from the claimed band to the calibrated outside outcome
(Definition 6.3.4a),

D\_op\^{}(outcome, e) = D( 𝒞\_o\^{}outcome, Bern(Q*\_e) ) = min\_\{c ∈
{[}C\_low, C\_high{]}\} JS( Bern(c), Bern(Q*\_e) ),

and its direction is recorded by splitting it:

D\_over,e = D\_op\^{}(outcome, e) if Q*\_e \textless{} C\_low, and 0
otherwise; D\_under,e = D\_op\^{}(outcome, e) if Q*\_e \textgreater{}
C\_high, and 0 otherwise.

The group values are D\_op\^{}outcome = max\_e D\_op\^{}(outcome, e),
D\_over = max\_e D\_over,e and D\_under = max\_e D\_under,e over
available e. The measures attaining them are recorded separately,
e*\_outcome, e*\_over and e*\_under (licensure first among ties),
because they can differ: licensure can attain the outcome group's value
through overclaiming while retention attains D\_under. {[}Model-derived:
the maximum across outside measures follows the no-weights rule of
Definition 6.3.4b and keeps retention, which covers far more students,
from swamping the stronger licensure evidence{]}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 15.2.12 (Properties of the Outcome Gap).}}

For each available e:

(a) D\_op\^{}(outcome, e) = JS( Bern(C\_low), Bern(Q*\_e) ) if Q*\_e
\textless{} C\_low; 0 if Q*\_e ∈ {[}C\_low, C\_high{]}; JS(
Bern(C\_high), Bern(Q*\_e) ) if Q*\_e \textgreater{} C\_high. It lies in
{[}0, 1{]}, and at most one of D\_over,e and D\_under,e is positive.

(b) It is zero exactly when Q*\_e lies in the band. Where τ\_out is
unreported, C\_high = 1, so D\_under,e = 0 always: underclaiming cannot
be detected without transfer data, and its absence there is not evidence
that it is absent.

(c) D\_over,e is strictly increasing as Q*\_e falls below C\_low, and
D\_under,e strictly increasing as Q*\_e rises above C\_high.

(d) Each direction is registered only at the end of the band least
favorable to it, so neither can be produced by the treatment of
transfers alone.

(e) Where τ\_out is reported as 0, the band is the point C\_rep and
D\_op\^{}(outcome, e) = JS( Bern(C\_rep), Bern(Q*\_e) ), the point-claim
form.

(f) D\_op\^{}(outcome, e) is continuously differentiable in (C\_low,
C\_high, Q*\_e) on the interior of the feasible domain \{0 \textless{}
C\_low ≤ C\_high ≤ 1, 0 \textless{} Q*\_e \textless{} 1\}, including
across the band's edges Q*\_e = C\_low and Q*\_e = C\_high; this is the
regularity Proposition 6.3.4e requires of a set claim that moves with
the state. On the boundary of that domain the function is well defined,
and its one-sided derivatives are finite at points where both arguments
of the attained divergence lie in (0, 1) or coincide; where an endpoint
attaining the divergence equals 1 while Q*\_e \textless{} 1 (in
particular at C\_low = C\_high = 1), the derivative with respect to that
endpoint is unbounded (Lemma 15.2.1), and the boundary provisions of
Proposition 6.3.4e (fixed smoothing or the derivative-free update)
apply.

{[}Derived{]}

Proof. (a) By Lemma 15.2.1, for fixed Q*\_e the map c ↦ JS(Bern(c),
Bern(Q*\_e)) is strictly decreasing for c \textless{} Q*\_e and strictly
increasing for c \textgreater{} Q*\_e, so over the band its minimum is
at the endpoint nearest Q*\_e, or 0 if Q*\_e is inside. The two
directions require Q*\_e \textless{} C\_low and Q*\_e \textgreater{}
C\_high ≥ C\_low, which cannot both hold. (b) is (a) with C\_high = 1,
since Q*\_e \textgreater{} 1 is impossible. (c) is Lemma 15.2.1 with the
arguments exchanged by symmetry. (d) restates the definition, since
C\_low ≤ C\_rep ≤ C\_high. (e) is (a) with C\_low = C\_high. (f) Each
piece of (a) is continuously differentiable on the interior; at Q*\_e =
C\_low, the outer piece and its partial derivatives vanish, because
JS(Bern(a), Bern(b)) has a zero gradient at a = b (Lemma 15.2.1), so the
pieces join with matching values and derivatives, and likewise at
C\_high. ∎

Features and reporting. D\_over and D\_under enter the v4 model as
separate features. The outcome group value D\_op\^{}outcome,
direction-free like every divergence of Definition 6.2, enters the
aggregate D\_op of Definition 6.3.4b through its elementary dimensions.
Overclaiming is the case the credential-inflation hypothesis is about:
completion claimed beyond what the outside measures support.
Underclaiming, outcomes stronger than reported completion even counting
every transfer as a success, is an official layer understating an
operational layer that is working, read against the medium-entropy
signature (prediction P4) and reported as a finding in its own right,
not pooled with overclaiming. Because underclaiming is detectable only
where transfer data exist (Proposition 15.2.12(b)), the availability of
τ\_out is a reported stratification variable in every analysis of
D\_under. Likewise, which outside measures are available is a
stratification variable in the principal outcome analyses, not only an
output field, since colleges with and without licensure data may differ
systematically.

Colleges without an outside measure. Where neither outcome dimension is
available, the college is not skipped: it is scored on its other
available dimensions (Definition 6.3.4b) and also reported as its own
group, with its closure rate. Skipping it would recreate tracker v1's
insufficient-data trap, in which the colleges with the thinnest records
were disproportionately the ones that closed.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.11a (The Transfer Gap).}}

For a transfer-mission college, the transfer claim is that the students
it sends on succeed: P\_o\^{}trn = Bern(1). Its realization is π\_succ,
the share of the college's transfers in the covered years who succeeded
at the receiving institution, from a post-transfer source reporting by
sending college (in 2026: the Texas Higher Education Coordinating
Board's per-pair reports; Virginia's SCHEV Transfer Feedback reports;
North Carolina's university-system transfer dashboard and the community
college system's college transfer performance measure; the University of
California's outcomes by source school, for UC-bound transfers only;
others as verified). Success is bachelor's completion within a window
fixed per source where the source reports it, and otherwise first-year
persistence at the receiving institution (good academic standing or
return the next fall), with the definition recorded; a change in a
source's definition starts a new comparability segment. The transfer gap
is

D\_op\^{}trn = g\_JS( 1 − π\_succ ) = JS( Bern(1), Bern(π\_succ) ).

c\_trn is the covered transfers in the source over the college's
transfer-outs in the same years, capped at 1, and is reported with every
value. ι\_trn(t) = 1 where ι\_tm = 1, the source covers at least
n\_min\^{}trn transfers (default 30; a threshold separate from the
licensure minimum n\_min, though with the same default) and c\_trn ≥
c\_min (default 0.5). State sources cover only transfers to that state's
public universities, so the state is a stratification variable in every
analysis using the transfer gap, and so that having transfer data is not
confused with being in a particular state. {[}Model-derived; the claim
is imposed by the adapter on transfer-mission colleges, as with
soundness{]}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 15.2.11b.}}

D\_op\^{}trn ∈ {[}0, 1{]}; it is 0 exactly when every covered transfer
succeeded and 1 exactly when none did, and strictly increasing in the
share who did not. A successful transfer therefore adds nothing to the
transfer gap and counts as a completion in the outcome band, while an
unsuccessful one shows here and counts as a non-completion there.
{[}Derived from Proposition 15.2.3 and Definition 15.2.9{]}

Small-sample sensitivity, a required validation. A maximum can be driven
by one noisy measure, so a noisy licensure rate can set the outcome
group's value while retention is quiet. Before the v4 protocol is fixed,
the channel-selection stages compare the outcome gap with licensure at
n\_min = 30 against higher minimums, and against licensure alone and
retention alone, on the development colleges, under a comparison fixed
in advance; whichever of these versions performs best under that
comparison is the outcome claim carried into v4, and the others are
reported. This comparison is part of dimension selection, the second of
the protocol's five steps: its criterion is fixed before it is run, and
it uses development colleges only.

\textbf{The four-year gap}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.13 (The Four-Year Gap).}}

For a college with a bachelor's-seeking cohort, let G\_100(t) and
G\_150(t) be the shares of the same cohort, on the same population and
reporting basis, completing within 100\% and 150\% of normal time (IPEDS
Graduation Rates), with G\_150(t) \textgreater{} 0. The on-time share
r\_on(t) = G\_100(t)/G\_150(t) ∈ {[}0, 1{]} is the share of students
completing within 150\% of normal time who complete within the stated
length, and the four-year gap is

D\_op\^{}4yr(t) = g\_JS( 1 − r\_on(t) ) = JS( Bern(1), Bern( r\_on(t) )
).

The claim is that the degree takes its stated length; the realization is
the on-time share among the college's completers within the 150\%
window. Taking the share among those completers, rather than the whole
cohort, keeps this dimension separate from the outcome gap: a college
whose students do not finish shows in the outcome gap, and one whose
students finish late shows here. Students completing after 150\% of
normal time are outside the denominator, so the gap understates delay at
a college with substantial completion beyond that window; where Outcome
Measures report awards at eight years, the effect of including them is
tested on the development colleges. ι\_4yr(t) = 1 where G\_150(t)
\textgreater{} 0 and the college's stated length for the cohort's
bachelor's programs is the normal time IPEDS uses. IPEDS measures
bachelor's cohorts against four years (to be verified against the data
dictionary before building), so a college whose programs are officially
longer (cooperative-education programs designed for five years, for
example) is marked unavailable on this dimension rather than scored
against a claim it does not make. Because the IPEDS cohort is
college-wide, availability is decided college-wide: a college whose
standard bachelor's length is longer than four years is unavailable, and
a college with only some longer programs (a five-year architecture
degree, for example) is scored and flagged, with the longer programs
named where the college's catalog states them. The flag is a
stratification variable in every four-year-gap analysis, since a
college-wide ratio over programs of different lengths mixes claims, and
every four-year-gap result is repeated without the flagged colleges as a
sensitivity analysis (and at program level where program-level
time-to-degree data exist). {[}Model-derived{]}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 15.2.14.}}

D\_op\^{}4yr ∈ {[}0, 1{]}; it is 0 exactly when every student completing
within 150\% of normal time completes within the stated length, 1
exactly when none of them does, and strictly increasing in the share of
them finishing late. {[}Derived from Proposition 15.2.3{]}

\textbf{The soundness gap and the v4 meta channel}

The adapter operationalizes an implicit institutional claim: that an
operating college is sound and will keep operating. This is a
presumption the model imposes on the official layer, not a statement
each college has published. Within a college's scoring window the claim
holds by construction: a public announcement of closure, merger or
financial exigency is an outcome that ends the window, not a reading
inside it. Its realization is read from the college's operating
coverage, a proxy for the claim rather than a complete measure of
financial soundness: a college can run a planned deficit while sound
(reserves, endowment, committed financing), and can cover its expenses
while unsound (debt, liquidity, deferred maintenance, pension
liabilities). The claim as measured is therefore one of operating
sustainability. The dimension keeps the name soundness, for the claim it
stands for, but every output labels its measured construct operating
sustainability (operating-coverage proxy), and D\_op\^{}sound is not
read as a measure of insolvency or of a college's whole financial
condition: it is divergence from an imposed operating-coverage claim.
The dimension is read with that limitation, and the meta-representation
layer's reading of it is revealed by insiders' behavior: people who can
see the operational reality and conclude the official claim is false
leave at a higher rate. This makes soundness the one dimension on which
the meta channel can be read from national data, because exit is
recorded for nearly every college and collected separately from the
finance and outcome files.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.15 (The Soundness Readings).}}

For a college at fiscal year t inside its scoring window:

Official: the claim of soundness, P\_o\^{}sound = Bern(1).

Operational: P\_p\^{}sound = Bern(s\_p), with s\_p(t) = min\{1,
B\_cov(t)\} and B\_cov(t) the ratio of total revenues to total expenses
(IPEDS Finance), defined where total expenses are reported and positive:
an operating-coverage proxy, under which a college covering its expenses
realizes the claim fully and one covering a fraction of them realizes
that fraction.

Meta: P\_m\^{}sound = Bern(s\_ins), the insiders' reading, from exit.
With FT(t) the full-time staff headcount (instructional and
non-instructional) in fall t and NH(t) the full-time new hires reported
for t (IPEDS Human Resources), the estimated exit rate is x\_exit(t) =
max\{0, NH(t) − (FT(t) − FT(t − 1))\} / FT(t − 1), defined where FT(t −
1) \textgreater{} 0. This is a residual under a headcount model, not an
observed count of departures: the change in full-time headcount also
moves with transfers between full-time and part-time status, transfers
between staff categories, reclassifications, changes in reporting
coverage, and differences between the hiring window and the fall
snapshot. x\_exit estimates unreplaced or otherwise unaccounted-for
staffing loss, and it does not separate voluntary departure from
involuntary separation. Its excess over the norm for comparable colleges
is x\_ex(t) = max\{0, x\_exit(t) − x\_norm\}, where x\_norm is the
median exit rate of development colleges in the same sector and the same
wealth band (terciles of endowment per student; appropriations per
student for public colleges), so that the norm itself, not only the
later analysis, removes sector composition. Cells and fallbacks, in this
fixed order: (1) a sector-and-band cell with fewer than m\_min
development colleges (default 20) is merged with its adjacent band in
the same sector, and where it has two adjacent bands, with the one
holding more development colleges (the lower band if they tie); (2) if
the merged cell is still below m\_min, all wealth bands of the sector
are pooled; (3) if the sector's pool is still below m\_min, the meta
reading is unavailable for that sector, with the reason recorded. The
rule is applied mechanically and fixed before any norm is computed. A
college with no wealth data is compared with its sector's pooled norm,
and flagged. Then

s\_ins(t) = 1 − min\{ 1, x\_ex(t) / x\_ref \},

with x\_ref the 95th percentile of x\_ex among development colleges of
the same sector. The reference is computed only where the sector has at
least n\_pos development colleges with positive excess exit (default
20), so that it does not rest on a handful of colleges; where it has
fewer, or the percentile is zero, the meta reading is unavailable for
that sector, with the reason recorded, rather than defined by an
arbitrary floor. The 90th and 97.5th percentiles are run as fixed
sensitivity alternatives and reported. x\_norm, x\_ref, the wealth bands
and the cell rules are fixed on the development colleges with the v4
protocol and then frozen.

ι\_sound(t) = 1 where B\_cov(t) is defined; the meta reading is
available (sound ∈ K\_m(t)) where FT(t), FT(t − 1) \textgreater{} 0 and
NH(t) are supplied and the college's sector has a valid norm and a valid
x\_ref. The meta reading is of the Estimated type (§15.2): a revealed
reading inferred from behavior under a stated model, not a direct
observation of what insiders believe. {[}Model-derived: the coverage
form of s\_p, the exit estimate and the linear map to s\_ins are each
chosen among alternatives; s\_ins is a revealed reading of the estimated
staffing residual, so it is a proxy for insiders' reading, not a
measurement of their beliefs{]}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 15.2.16 (The Soundness Divergences).}}

With the readings of Definition 15.2.15,

D\_op\^{}sound = g\_JS( 1 − s\_p ), D\_om\^{}sound = g\_JS( 1 − s\_ins
), D\_pm\^{}sound = JS( Bern(s\_p), Bern(s\_ins) ).

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 15.2.17 (What the Soundness Divergences
Read).}}

(a) D\_op\^{}sound is 0 exactly when revenue covers expenses and rises
strictly as coverage falls; D\_om\^{}sound is 0 exactly when exit is at
or below the norm for comparable colleges and rises strictly with excess
exit up to the reference level. (b) Because the official reading is
fixed, D\_op\^{}sound and D\_om\^{}sound are each a monotone function of
one input; the information the meta channel adds is D\_pm\^{}sound,
which is 0 exactly when the staffing-based proxy and the
operating-coverage proxy agree and grows as they diverge, in either
direction. (c) s\_ins \textless{} s\_p (estimated staffing loss beyond
what coverage alone would explain) and s\_ins \textgreater{} s\_p
(staffing holding while coverage falls) are distinguished by the sign of
s\_p − s\_ins, recorded alongside D\_pm\^{}sound. (d) Proposition
6.3.4d(b) holds on this dimension: D\_op\^{}sound ≤ 2·( D\_om\^{}sound +
D\_pm\^{}sound ) for the base-2 Jensen--Shannon divergence (ρ\_Δ = 2).
{[}Derived{]}

Proof. (a) Proposition 15.2.3, since 1 − s\_p and 1 − s\_ins are
nondecreasing in the shortfall and the excess exit, strictly where they
are below 1. (b) P\_o\^{}sound is constant, so D\_op\^{}sound depends on
s\_p alone and D\_om\^{}sound on s\_ins alone; D\_pm\^{}sound = 0
exactly when s\_p = s\_ins and is strictly increasing in \textbar s\_p −
s\_ins\textbar{} for either fixed argument (Lemma 15.2.1). (c) is a
recording rule. (d) is Proposition 6.3.4d(b) with the point claim
Bern(1), and Jensen--Shannon divergence has ρ\_Δ = 2 (Spine §6.3.3). ∎

The v4 channels. On the soundness dimension tracker v4 runs in
three-channel mode. D\_pm\^{}sound measures the discrepancy between the
inferred staffing-based proxy and the operating-coverage proxy, and
D\_om\^{}sound the departure of the staffing-based proxy from the
imposed claim. Reading these as insiders' perception of the college, and
as insiders ceasing to believe its official claim, is the hypothesis the
dimension is built to test (P6), not something the data establish. On
the price, outcome and four-year dimensions no meta reading is defined,
so they are in two-channel status (Definition 6.3.4b), and their
divergences are labeled so. External governance attestations, AAUP
censures and accreditor actions citing governance standards, enter
separately under Spine Definition 6.1.1, as an override, not a weighted
input. Trigger: a censure or such an action in force in fiscal year t.
Effective period: every year in which it remains in force. Release:
removal from the AAUP censure list, or the accreditor's record that the
action is resolved, effective from the following fiscal year;
hand-collected voice (no-confidence votes, lawsuits) is added only once
its records are shown to be clean enough. Because the
administrative-layer tests found that wealth drives staffing shares, the
exit norm is set within sector and wealth band, and every analysis of
the soundness meta reading is also stratified by sector, with
sensitivity to the norm's cells reported.

\textbf{The claim set for tracker v4}

The v4 claim set is K\_cl(t) ⊆ \{price, (outcome, lic), (outcome, ret),
trn, 4yr, sound\}, with K\_m(t) ⊆ \{sound\}, aggregated by Definition
6.3.4b. For every college and year the outputs give every dimensional
value, the group values, D\_over and D\_under with the outside measure
attaining each, the sign of s\_p − s\_ins, q*\_op and the attaining
measure, K\_cl(t), K\_m(t), the unclipped r\_raw, the availability of
τ\_out, and, at transfer-mission colleges, π\_succ, c\_trn and the
post-transfer source. Each dimension is tested alone and in combination
on the development colleges before the v4 protocol is fixed (the
channel-selection stages), so the dimensions that enter v4 are chosen on
development data, never on test outcomes. The protocol separates five
steps, in order, each completed and committed before the next begins:
fitting the calibrations (δ\_need, h\_e, x\_norm, x\_ref); selecting the
dimensions; fixing the thresholds; fixing the prediction models; and
only then scoring and evaluating the held-out test colleges, whose
outcomes are looked up after the scores are committed. Within the
development colleges the calibrations are fitted on the earlier years
and the selections evaluated on the later years. Because the development
colleges are used for several rounds of selection, performance measured
on them is selection-stage performance, used only to choose, and is
never presented as an estimate of predictive performance; the held-out
test is the evaluation. The primary outcome is the v4 verdict under pass
rules carried over from the v2 and v3 protocols; P1 to P6 are secondary
hypotheses, each with one primary endpoint (P1, P2 and P5: distress in
the outcome window; P3: test (a); P4: the signature comparison; P6: the
combined deterioration endpoint), tested with a Holm correction across
the six at a family-wise level of 0.05; every other result, including
P3(b), P4's recovery and reorganization rates and P6's separate
endpoints, is reported as descriptive.

Comparing colleges with different available sets. Within one college,
aggregate trends are segmented when the available set changes (§6.3.4).
Across colleges, an aggregate taken over fewer dimensions has fewer
chances to be high, so the protocol fixes in advance that cross-college
comparisons of aggregate levels are made on the common available set
(colleges with every group applicable to them available) as the primary
analysis, with the analysis repeated within each available-set stratum
and reported. Because colleges with every group available may differ
from the rest, every primary analysis reports how that subset differs
from the full sample in sector, size, wealth and closure rate.

\textbf{Predictions}

Shared operational definitions, fixed with the v4 protocol on the
development colleges before any test college is scored. Distress:
closure, merger or deletion in the outcome window, coded as in the v2
and v3 tests, with the three components always also reported separately;
a deletion is adjudicated against the IPEDS header files (close date,
successor identifier) and coded a closure only where the institution
ceased operating, otherwise an administrative change, reported apart. A
claim group g is diverging at t when its value exceeds θ\_g, the 75th
percentile of that group's values among development colleges of the same
sector in the baseline years (default; the percentile is fixed in the
protocol). Widening of a series: a positive least-squares slope over the
three fiscal years before the outcome window, computed within one
comparability segment of that series; each prediction names the series.
Holding a quantity constant means including it as a covariate in the
fixed outcome model of the protocol, with stratification as a reported
check. Each prediction is tested at equal size and wealth and within
sectors where the sample allows, and every prediction's interpretation
keeps the construct limitations of the measures it uses.

P1 {[}Hypothesis{]}. Colleges whose excess discount widened since their
baseline (Δ\_ex at the last year before the outcome window above the
75th percentile of development colleges of the same sector), with the
published price not cut (S\_post at that year, in nominal dollars and
computed with the in-state and out-of-state weights of t₀ so that a
shift in the student mix does not count as a price change, at least its
value at t₀; nominal because a nominal reduction is what a college
announces as a price cut, with an inflation-adjusted version reported as
a sensitivity check; a change in which fees count as required, or in the
published-price definition, starts a new comparability segment), are
distressed more often than other colleges, most of all among
tuition-dependent private colleges: the official price is maintained
while the price realized erodes beneath it. P1 concerns widening since
baseline, not a continuing slope; a college that widened and then held
flat counts. The price gap's limitation applies: the widening is
relative to comparable practice.

P2 {[}Hypothesis{]}. Colleges whose overclaiming widened (the outcome
group diverging with D\_over attaining it, and the series D\_over
widening under the shared definition) are distressed more often than
colleges whose outcome gap held or narrowed. D\_over measures departure
from the development sample's relationship between outside measures and
reported completion, not from an independently verified completion rate.

P3 {[}Hypothesis{]}. Two separate tests. (a) The number of claim groups
diverging at once predicts distress, with risk rising with the count;
the primary analysis is restricted to colleges with every group
applicable to them available, and a secondary analysis uses the share of
available groups diverging, with the number available as a covariate.
(b) Among colleges diverging on exactly one group, which group it is
predicts the kind of ending (linked to the endings test); a group with
fewer than 10 such colleges is reported descriptively and left out of
the comparison. (a) asks whether divergence accumulates; (b) whether its
domain matters.

P4 {[}Hypothesis{]}. Underclaiming colleges (D\_under \textgreater{} 0
at the index year) show the medium-entropy signature of Catalog
§10.5b.1, volatility carried by the official layer, more often than
overclaiming colleges, and, consistent with Catalog §10.5b.2's account
of medium-entropy containers, recover or reorganize more readily if
distressed. Index year: t\_I, the final eligible year before the outcome
window; each college is classified at t\_I into one of four mutually
exclusive groups: underclaiming only (D\_under \textgreater{} 0, D\_over
= 0), overclaiming only (D\_over \textgreater{} 0, D\_under = 0), mixed
(both positive, possible because the two can be attained by different
outside measures on opposite sides of the band), and neither. The
directional comparison is between the underclaiming-only and
overclaiming-only groups; mixed colleges are reported separately and
enter neither group. The attaining measure e*\_under (or e*\_over) is
recorded at t\_I and held fixed for the whole comparison, so the series
compared is one measure throughout, never measures stitched together
across years. Instantiation: the official series is C\_low, the lower
end of the reported completion band (which is C\_rep wherever c\_trn =
0, and includes covered transfer successes at transfer-mission
colleges), so that it shares a base with Q*\_e, which is calibrated
against C\_low (Definition 15.2.10); the operational series is Q*\_e for
that fixed e, over the volatility window ending at t\_I; years in which
that measure is unavailable are left out, never filled from the other
measure. The two variances are computed over the same matched annual
intervals: an interval counts only where both C\_low and the fixed Q*\_e
have valid observations at both of its ends, so that unequal observation
windows cannot produce the signature. The signature is present when the
variance of the matched year-to-year changes in C\_low exceeds that in
Q*\_e over at least four consecutive matched changes within one
comparability segment; a college without four such changes is excluded
from the signature test and counted in a reported exclusion tally. A
version using the outcome group's own attaining measure e*\_outcome at
t\_I is reported separately and does not replace it. Distress for this
test is operating distress: coverage B\_cov below 1 for two consecutive
years. Recovery: B\_cov at or above 1 for two consecutive years within
five years of the distress signal, without closure. Reorganization: a
merger or change of control recorded in the IPEDS header files within
the same five years, with the institution continuing to operate.
Recovery and reorganization are reported as separate outcomes, never
combined into one favorable outcome. Denominators: for each directional
group, the colleges that show the operating-distress signal after t\_I
and have the full five years of follow-up after it; a college that
closes during follow-up stays in the denominator as neither recovered
nor reorganized, and a college whose follow-up is cut short by the end
of the data is excluded and counted in the reported exclusion tally.
Tested only among colleges with a reported, cohort-aligned τ\_out
(Definition 15.2.9) at t\_I, so that underclaiming is observable;
post-transfer outcomes are not required, and where they are present,
c\_trn is reported.

P5 {[}Hypothesis{]}. A widening four-year gap (the series D\_op\^{}4yr
widening) predicts distress with the outcome group's value held
constant, the covariate being D\_op\^{}outcome at the last year of the
slope window, before the outcome window, so that no outcome-window
information enters.

P6 {[}Hypothesis{]}. The estimated insider-exit signal leads operating
deterioration. Signal at year t: s\_ins \textless{} s\_p with
D\_pm\^{}sound above the 75th percentile of development colleges of the
same sector, in two consecutive years ending at t. Operating
deterioration: a negative least-squares slope of B\_cov over years t + 1
to t + 3, or B\_cov falling below 1 in any of those years from at or
above 1 at t. Comparison group: colleges of the same sector and wealth
band without the signal at t, assigned at t and held fixed for the
forward test even if a comparison college develops the signal later. The
slope endpoint requires B\_cov in all three years t + 1 to t + 3, with
no interpolation; a college missing any of them is excluded from that
endpoint and counted, and the crossing endpoint uses the years
available. The two deterioration endpoints (a negative slope; a crossing
below break-even) are reported separately as well as combined, since a
college can decline while well above break-even, or cross it without a
negative three-year slope. Distress in the outcome window is tested the
same way. Because x\_exit is a staffing residual, not an observed count
of voluntary departures, P6 tests whether the estimated exit signal
leads operating deterioration; it does not test directly whether
insiders know the college is failing. The residual also moves with
retirement waves, competition in the local labor market, unionization,
wage differences, changes in staffing composition and reorganizations,
none of which is a reading of the college's condition; these limits are
carried into the reporting of P6 alongside any result.

\hypertarget{engine-reference-every-derived-quantity-one-formula-each}{%
\subsection{15.3 Engine Reference --- Every Derived Quantity, One
Formula
Each}\label{engine-reference-every-derived-quantity-one-formula-each}}

Organized by originating Part. Every entry in the rightmost column is a
free parameter analogous to a, b, c, d, e, r in that earlier
single-container application --- not guessed, not fixed by the theory,
and exactly what the Bayesian state-space layer exists to fit once real
data and a chosen system are in hand.

\hypertarget{a-type-and-units-ledger}{%
\subsection{15.3a Type and Units Ledger}\label{a-type-and-units-ledger}}

Every state variable, rate, accumulated quantity and forcing term used
across Parts 4 to 12, with its mathematical type, its range or units,
and where it is defined. A quantity used in a way its entry does not
permit (a stock compared with a rate, a vector compared with a scalar,
an unnormalized divergence where a fraction is required) is an error at
the interface between sections, and the ledger exists to make such
errors visible.

\hypertarget{what-happens-next-deliberately-not-done-in-this-document}{%
\subsection{15.4 What Happens Next (Deliberately Not Done in This
Document)}\label{what-happens-next-deliberately-not-done-in-this-document}}

This document stops short of choosing Ω, writing the adapter for any
specific system, or fitting any of the coefficients in §15.3 to real
data --- that work is domain-specific by construction (§15.1) and comes
after the mathematics is settled, not before. The next step is choosing
a system, defining its adapter (§15.2) concretely, and building the
Bayesian state-space model that infers §15.3's fitted parameters from
that system's data --- a PyMC layer for RICD's full parameter set, built
so that every latent variable (I, S, d\_A, P\_C, V, Z, Ω\_hazard, and
the valve-symmetry quantities) is computed by exactly one formula
throughout, used identically in the generative model, the inference
likelihood, and any downstream simulation.

\textbf{Final Statement}

Recursive Information--Container Dynamics advances the proposition that
adaptive systems, despite their immense diversity, can be described
within a common mathematical framework in which recursively organized
information-processing containers evolve through the coupled dynamics of
information, resources, memory, boundaries, and networks. Within this
framework, persistence is the preservation of adaptive possibility,
collapse is its contraction, and recovery is its expansion. Every
quantity in that framework is either computed from real data through a
stated formula, supplied through an explicit adapter-supplied input, or
explicitly returned as unavailable/undefined when its required
evidentiary conditions do not exist (as with G(t) absent a genuinely
independent external party, §6.3.2) --- every symbol denotes exactly one
quantity, the structural form a container takes is itself derived from
the same informational dynamics that govern everything else in the
theory, and the meta-representation layer's two directions of
transmission are tracked as two independent, comparably-measured
quantities rather than one --- the theory and the tool are the same
object.
